2026.11.10 早报

覆盖窗口：2026-11-09T06:00:00+08:00 → 2026-11-10T06:00:00+08:00 · 更新时间：2026.11.10 06:17 北京时间 · 317 条

窗口：2026-11-09 06:00 至 2026-11-10 06:00（北京时间，UTC+8）｜监控名单 134 个账号｜所有时间均为北京时间。

Executive Summary｜核心结论

本期窗口为 2026-11-09 06:00 至 2026-11-10 06:00（北京时间，UTC+8），覆盖周一亚洲与美股交易日。主线：（1）Nvidia / 黄仁勋事件进入董事会独立调查与国会审查阶段，焦点从个人丑闻转向“对华 GPU 敞口”；（2）Anthropic 发布 Claude Fable 5.2 / Mythos 5.2 并据报启动 IPO 推介；（3）AI 基础设施的融资与电力约束被重新定价（CDS、担保、发电机、德州许可冻结）；（4）存储/半导体链条出现 Terafab 巨量产能目标、Lam Research 涉嫌泄密、台系 DRAM 购入 EUV 等新变量。以下“分析/含义”为本摘要的综合判断；凡标注“据报/传闻/作者观点”的内容均来自 X 帖子或其转述的媒体报道，未经独立核实，数字均以原帖为准。

一、Nvidia：从“CEO 丑闻”转为“对华敞口审查”，基本面风险在国会程序

事实层（转述）：@GavinSBaker 转述 Nvidia 董事会声明——“没有理由相信”国家安全或公司机密遭泄露，启动由独立董事委员会监督、外部律师协助的独立调查，黄仁勋表示全力配合。@jukan05 转述陆行之认为美方借丑闻推进削弱黄仁勋反制裁游说、收紧 H200 对华许可与东南亚渠道等目标。市场端：@aleabitoreddit 称 TAIEX 周一 +4.94%（史上第二大单日涨幅）；@benitoz 转述 Bernstein Stacy Rasgon 认为会带来政治/监管噪音但不会实质改变需求；@ScroogeCap 转述 Mizuho Jordan Klein：按买方 EPS 预期 CY28 市盈率仅 7–8 倍，是更坚定的买家。

观点：@dylan522p 认为未来 6 个月事件会在媒体/金融圈平息，但民主党会在国会彻查对华 GPU 销售，“另一只靴子”可能那时落地；@zephyr_z9 估计合法销售 + 境外租用 + 走私合计约占 Nvidia 需求 ~20%，并认为禁止中国公司境外租用算力将重创现货市场，且可能回归 AI diffusion rule；@GavinSBaker 则指出自 2025 年夏以来更多是北京在阻止 Nvidia GPU 入华（约 60 万颗 H200 滞留香港，据路透）。

含义：NVDA 的风险不在 CEO 去留，而在中国相关需求（含东南亚租用）在国会与出口管制程序中被压缩，以及对 GPU 租赁/现货价格和东南亚 neocloud 的二阶影响；估值（7–8x CY28）已计入相当多 AI 不确定性。跟踪：听证时间表、境外租用规则、H200 许可。

二、Anthropic：Fable 5.2 登顶 + IPO 推介，单价降但每任务成本升

事实层：Anthropic 发布 Claude Fable 5.2 与 Mythos 5.2（@kimmonismus、@PhotonCap）：定价每百万 token 8/40 美元，较 Fable 5.1 低 20%，输出快最多 30%。@ArtificialAnlys：Fable 5.2（max）以 65 分登顶 AA 智能指数 v5（Gemini 4 Carbon 60、Opus 5.5 59、GPT-6 Astra 57），但 max 下平均每任务成本 26.5 美元，约为 Opus 5.5 的 2.6 倍。@EpochAIResearch：ECI 174 创纪录（此前 GPT-6 Astra 169）。@AndrewCurran_ 引述：合并进 Anthropic 核心代码库的变更中 >90% 由 Claude 编写。

IPO（据报）：@kimmonismus 转述彭博称最早周二启动推介、下一周在纳斯达克上市，部分投资者讨论 1.1–1.3 万亿美元估值；@PhotonCap 称目标 1–1.5 万亿美元，低于此前 2 万亿美元的说法；@AndrewCurran_ 称股票计划下周四或周五开始交易。关键词扫描中，第三方合成市场报价 Anthropic 约 1.59–1.70 万亿美元、OpenAI 约 1.35–1.37 万亿美元（非公开市场价格）。

含义：前沿能力领先 + IPO 时点叠加，Anthropic 是本周 AI 资产定价的锚；“token 单价下降、每任务成本上升”意味着推理算力需求（token 用量）仍在放大，利好算力/存储链，但对下游应用毛利是压力。同日 Meta Muse Ember（AA 36 分，与 Haiku 5.5 持平、每任务成本约 1/3）显示中低档模型价格战继续。

三、AI 基础设施：需求未变，风险在“融资方”与“电力/许可”

@nodiligence：民主党拿下国会后 SpaceX、CoreWeave CDS 创纪录，而 hyperscaler 保护成本下降——市场在区分“买单方”和“融资方”：Broadcom 为 Anthropic 芯片融资提供约 720 亿美元兜底，AMD 对合作伙伴数据中心的担保一个季度内从 41 亿美元升至 77 亿美元；Oracle 200 亿美元发债利差比 2 月多付最多 175bp。另：Anthropic 以 34 亿美元买下 1.7GW Caterpillar 发电机（约 2000 美元/kW，相当于约 7 周算力收入），Google 把 Crusoe 挤出怀俄明园区并计划投入 170 亿美元自建。

@SemiAnalysis_：德州 9 月底已停止发放数据中心 TCEQ 许可，ERCOT 自 8 月起做并网审计，德州约占 2028 年美国新增容量近 30%；@nodiligence 引 UBS 估计 2028 年计划容量约 30% 可能被暂停。@FundaAI 周报：长期乐观，但中期（至 2027）可能出现投资者预期支出与客户实际兑现之间的差距，亚洲供应链交付/采购放缓报告增多（尚不能证明砍单）。@steph_palazzolo：推理服务商 Fireworks 遭黑客攻击、客户数据泄露，部分客户考虑迁出。

含义：电力与许可成为 2027–28 年容量的真正约束，利好掌握电力/场地的一方（hyperscaler 自建、发电设备厂商），而为租约做担保或以项目抵押借款的 neocloud/开发商/芯片厂商承担尾部风险；AVGO、AMD 的表外担保值得纳入风险评估。

四、半导体与存储：周期强劲，供给侧变量增多

@jukan05 / @dnystedt 转述日经：Musk 的 Terafab 告知日本设备商目标 2032 年起每月 100 万片晶圆（70 万片存储、30 万片逻辑），2027 年建试产线，要求非常规（定制工具、驻场工程）。@jukan05 转述韩媒：Lam Research 韩国公司涉嫌泄露三星蚀刻机密，或需赔偿约 1 万亿韩元（Jukan Terminal）。南亚科、华邦电计划购入 EUV 开发先进 DRAM；台积电侯永清称同时建 20 座厂已到极限、无法建 30 座；三井化学以 1.84 万亿日元收购 JSR；@SemiAnalysis_ 关注长鑫 4F² DRAM 送样。

关键词扫描（名单外，转述为主）：Counterpoint 称 3Q26 全球 DRAM 收入近 2000 亿美元（+369%），增长几乎全来自价格（比特 +4%）；JPM 称存储股远期 P/E 约 3.5x，超配，但预计电网瓶颈令 2027H1 订单显著放缓；SK 海力士在进博会称短缺或持续至 2028 年；外资 11 月 9 日仍净卖出三星、SK 海力士。软银与 Grab、PETROS 宣布 18.5 亿美元马来西亚 AI 基础设施种子资本（软银 10 亿美元），软银 11 月 10 日发布财报。

含义：存储处于“价格驱动”的超级周期，市场以 2–3.5x 远期盈利定价见顶风险；分歧点在 2027 年电力约束是否传导为订单放缓。Terafab 若部分兑现属长期供给威胁，但时间表激进、可信度待验证；对日本设备商短期是订单利好。

Highlights / Top 10｜按投资重要性排序

#1 @GavinSBaker · 2026-11-09 08:41 北京时间

要点：Nvidia 董事会今天表示，“没有理由相信”黄仁勋的行为导致国家安全或 Nvidia 机密遭到泄露。

他们正在启动一项由独立董事组成的委员会监督、并由外部独立法律顾问协助的独立调查，审查所有可获得的信息。鉴于这些独立董事负有的受托责任，这将是一项公正、详尽而彻底的调查。

黄仁勋表示：“我支持这项调查，并将全力配合。我热爱我们的国家和我们的公司。我致力于保护 NVIDIA、支持美国的国家安全。我为身为美国人感到自豪和感激。”

我认识 Jensen 将近 30 年了。我根本不相信他会明知故犯地做任何对美国或 Nvidia 不利的事情。

他对我谈到中国时唯一说过的，就是保持以 Nvidia 为首的美国硬件生态领先于中国是绝对重要的。仅此而已。

更详细的想法见下文：

为何重要：Nvidia 董事会正式表态并启动独立调查，是事件走向程序化的官方节点，决定 NVDA 管理层风险的边界。

#7 @nodiligence · 2026-11-09 20:16 北京时间

要点：$AVGO $AMD $MSFT $ORCL $SPCX $CRWV $META $GOOGL

民主党拿下国会两天后，SpaceX 和 CoreWeave 的 CDS 创下纪录，而彭博报道大型 hyperscaler 的保护成本反而下降了。所以市场已经在区分为 AI 建设买单的公司和为其提供融资的公司。

看看现在谁在承担风险，这有点道理：
——Broadcom 为 Anthropic 的芯片融资提供了约 720 亿美元的兜底，
——AMD 对合作伙伴数据中心的担保在一个季度内从 41 亿美元增至 77 亿美元。

路透社引用 UBS 估计，美国计划在 2028 年投运的数据中心容量中约 30% 可能被暂停。

如果发生这种情况，我认为 Microsoft 或 Meta 只是少花点钱，但为租约提供担保或以建设……（全文见下方分作者部分）

为何重要：用 CDS 与担保数据拆解 AI 建设的风险承担方：AVGO 720 亿美元兜底、AMD 担保 41→77 亿美元，对信用与股权风险评估都有用。

#8 @SemiAnalysis_ · 2026-11-10 03:58 北京时间

要点：美国数据中心建设的最大风险已经在一个州生效。

“最大的单一风险不是某个候选人，因为在许多情况下两党及候选人都希望开发商接受更严格的条件。”

“以德州为例，冻结已经存在。德州在 9 月底停止向数据中心项目发放 TCEQ 许可，此外 ERCOT 从 8 月起开始进行并网审计。”

“仅德州就占 2028 年美国新增容量的近 30%。所以仅这一个州、这一个市场损失几个月时间，就可能改变全国数字。”

为何重要：德州许可冻结约影响 2028 年美国新增容量近 30%，电力/许可成为数据中心建设的硬约束。

【二级市场投资人】

@GavinSBaker（保留 8 条 / 窗口内原创及回复 9 条）

2026-11-09 08:41 北京时间 ｜ Source: @GavinSBaker ｜ 原帖链接

Nvidia 董事会今天表示，“没有理由相信”黄仁勋的行为导致国家安全或 Nvidia 机密遭到泄露。

他们正在启动一项由独立董事组成的委员会监督、并由外部独立法律顾问协助的独立调查，审查所有可获得的信息。鉴于这些独立董事负有的受托责任，这将是一项公正、详尽而彻底的调查。

黄仁勋表示：“我支持这项调查，并将全力配合。我热爱我们的国家和我们的公司。我致力于保护 NVIDIA、支持美国的国家安全。我为身为美国人感到自豪和感激。”

我认识 Jensen 将近 30 年了。我根本不相信他会明知故犯地做任何对美国或 Nvidia 不利的事情。

他对我谈到中国时唯一说过的，就是保持以 Nvidia 为首的美国硬件生态领先于中国是绝对重要的。仅此而已。

更详细的想法见下文：

原文：Nvidia’s board stated today that it has found “no reason to believe that either national security or Nvidia secrets were compromised” as a result of Jensen’s actions.

They are initiating an independent investigation overseen by a committee of independent directors, assisted by outside independent counsel, that will examine all available information. This will be an unbiased, exhaustive and thorough investigation given the fiduciary responsibilities of these independent directors.

Jensen said that “I support the investigation and will cooperate fully. I love our country and our company. I am committed to protecting NVIDIA and supporting America’s national security. I am proud and grateful to be an American.”

I have known Jensen for almost 30 years. I simply do not believe he would ever knowingly do anything to disadvantage either America or Nvidia.

The only thing he has ever said to me about China is the absolute importance of keeping the American hardware ecosystem, led by Nvidia, ahead of China. That’s it.

More detailed thoughts follow below:

2026-11-09 08:41 北京时间 ｜ Source: @GavinSBaker ｜ 原帖链接

Jensen 曾说，在 Nvidia GPU 方面，美国应该拥有“最先、最多、最好”的。在这些 GPU 首先在美国推出很久之后，他一贯主张只向中国提供性能较弱的版本。

在我看来，这似乎是美国最好的国防政策：它降低了华为发展出有竞争力的 AI 硬件生态的几率，同时保持美国领先中国。允许与中国军方无关的中国 AI 实验室在东南亚使用云服务，与这一思路一致。一旦与中国爆发全面敌对，所有这些都可以瞬间关闭，这将在任何冲突中给美国带来显著优势。

从定义上说，如果中国拥有一个强健的、由华为主导的替代 AI 硬件生态，我们就不会有这种优势。而完全切断中国获取美国硬件的渠道，最终会催生另一个生态——鉴于中国电力过剩，这个生态不会针对能效优化。

中国正在开发大型光互连 scale-up 域，这需要中国大量拥有的激光衬底和电力。这些光学 scale-up 域可以在系统层面抵消美国单颗 GPU 的大部分优势，代价是消耗多得多的电力。华为 Atlas 960e 的光学 scale-up 域达 4096 颗芯片，而 NVL72 只有 72 颗 GPU。给中国 1）有限地获取落后于美国的 GPU，以及 2）在友好亚洲国家使用 GPU 云服务，能够拖慢中国的这一努力，因为这削弱了中国实验室投资于利用这种截然不同系统架构所需的软件和替代模型架构的动力。

理性的人可以对此有不同看法，但需要注意，拜登和特朗普两届政府中都有官员支持这一政策，也都有官员反对。把它当作一个简单问题对所有人都是一种伤害，这也是为什么 Jensen 所倡导的政策既有两党支持，也有两党反对。

这一切也正是中国限制获取 Nvidia GPU 的原因。中国理解上述所有逻辑，希望发展自己的、在冲突中无法被关闭的替代 AI 生态。中国实验室经常向投资者抱怨自己“GPU 匮乏”，并以在本国政府实际上捆住其手脚的情况下仍具竞争力为傲。我不确定中国希望从这次间谍行动中得到什么——毕竟他们自己在限制 Nvidia GPU 在中国境内的销售——但值得考虑的是，让 Jensen 被撤下 Nvidia CEO 之位可能正符合中国自身的国家安全目标。

Jensen 向家人和同事表达了深深的歉意，并对自己的行为承担全部责任。我认为明智的做法是在董事会调查完成之前暂缓进一步判断。

从个人层面说，Jensen 多年来一直是我的朋友。我所认识的他始终是爱国且深深亲美的。他*总是*说，他的故事只有在美国才可能发生。

原文：Jensen has said that America should have “the first, the most and the best” when it comes to Nvidia’s GPUs. He has consistently advocated for giving China less powerful versions of those GPUs long after they were first available in America.

From my perspective, this seems like the best national defense policy for America as it lowers the odds of Huawei developing a competitive AI hardware ecosystem while keeping America ahead of China. Letting Chinese AI labs that are not affiliated with the Chinese military use cloud services in Southeast Asia is consonant with this. All of this can be turned off in an instant in the event of outright hostilities with China which would give America a significant advantage in any conflict.

Definitionally, we would not have this advantage if China had a robust, Huawei led alternative AI hardware ecosystem. And completely restricting China’s access to American hardware would eventually result in the development of a different ecosystem that was not optimized for power efficiency given China’s surfeit of power.

China is working on developing large optical scale-up domains, which require the laser substrates and power that China has in abundance. These optical scale-up domains can offset much of the superiority of individual American GPUs at a system level at the cost of consuming dramatically more power. The Huawei Atlas 960e has a 4096 chip optical scale-up domains vs. the 72 GPU scale-up domain in the NVL72. Giving China 1) limited access to GPUs inferior to those available in America and 2) China access to GPU cloud services in friendly Asian countries stalls this Chinese effort by disincentivizing Chinese labs from investing in the software and alternative model architectures required to exploit this very different system architecture.

Reasonable minds can disagree about this, but important to note that officials in both the Biden and Trump administrations supported this policy and likewise officials in both administrations opposed it. Pretending that it is a simple question does everyone a disservice, which is why there is both bipartisan support and bipartisan opposition to the policy that Jensen has advocated.

And all of this is why China restricts access to Nvidia GPUs. China understands all of the above and wants to develop its own alternative AI ecosystem that cannot be turned off in the event of a conflict. Chinese labs regularly complain to investors about being “GPU poor” and take pride in the fact that they are competitive despite their own government effectively tying their hands. I’m not sure what China hoped to gain from this espionage operation given they are restricting sales of Nvidia GPUs inside of China, but it is worth considering that having Jensen removed as the CEO of Nvidia might serve China’s own national security goals.

Jensen expressed deep regret to his family and his colleagues for his actions while taking full responsibility for them. I think it is wise to defer further judgment until the board’s investigation has been completed.

On a personal level, Jensen has been a friend of mine for many long years. I have never known him to be anything other than patriotic and deeply pro-American. He *always* says that his story would have only been possible in America.

They are initiating an independent investigation overseen by a committee of independent directors, assisted by 链接

2026-11-10 05:43 北京时间 ｜ Source: @GavinSBaker ｜ 原帖链接

有件事值得那些认为拜登和特朗普两届政府允许向中国出口性能较弱 GPU 是错误的人更多讨论：

自 2025 年夏天以来，越来越多是*中国*、而不只是华盛顿在阻止 Nvidia GPU 进入中国。在商务部长 Lutnick 说目标是让中国开发者对美国技术栈“上瘾”后的几周内，北京就开始施压本国科技巨头停止购买 H20。随后又命令它们停止购买 RTX Pro 6000D，派海关团队到港口打击 Nvidia 芯片进口，并禁止新的国家资助数据中心使用外国 AI 芯片。而截至上周，据路透社报道，约 60 万颗 H200 滞留在香港，因为北京不批准大规模进口一款仍然优于中国本土产业大规模生产的任何产品的芯片。

如果允许出口这些较弱的 GPU 是一个*有利于*中国的政策错误，那为什么阻止它们的是*中国*？

在我看来，中国明白：如果它依赖美国 AI 硬件——无论是在中国本土，还是通过各种东南亚云（包括美国最大 hyperscaler 在东南亚的可用区）远程访问——那么在未来任何冲突中我们都拥有巨大的筹码。能够瞬间关闭云，实际上才是最大的筹码。

是的，中国 AI 实验室仍然想要 Nvidia 芯片。过去一年 H200 服务器的灰色市场价格大约涨了两倍。但这恰恰说明了问题。北京愿意让本国 AI 实验室付出真实代价来避免依赖美国 GPU，这说明北京如何看待 GPU 进口：不是礼物，而是对其自身雄心的威胁。

也许，只是也许，向中国出售落后于我们最先进水平的 GPU，让中国开发者对美国 GPU 保持“上瘾”，使中国不去建立一个我们无法控制或影响、*且*中国可能随时间与我们的对手共享的替代 AI 硬件生态，其实是一项聪明的、亲美的国防政策。而让他们使用东南亚云可能是更聪明的政策，因为云很容易关闭。

我过去相信、现在仍然相信这是聪明的政策。而且我这么说时，我在这里的帖子对华鹰派到我都不会再去那里旅行了。

请注意，拜登和特朗普两届政府都出于上述原因允许出口性能较弱的 GPU。只是在特朗普政府时期，中国才意识到这项其实相当聪明、有利于国防的政策的风险，然后开始限制 GPU。

H20 的教训是，刻意阉割的芯片并不能让中国留在我们的技术栈上。北京之所以对 H20 和 RTX Pro 6000D 动手，是因为它判断国产芯片已经追上了它们。所以，如果中国发展出一个与俄罗斯、伊朗和朝鲜共享的替代 AI 硬件生态，我们回头会认为，在 2025 年向中国提供 B30A，而不是最终逐案批准的 H200，会更加明智。尤其是 H200 并非前沿：随着 Vera Rubin 已全面量产，它比美国实验室正在购买的产品落后两代。

总而言之，Jensen 一直在游说的立场被拜登*和*特朗普两届政府采纳，*而且*随着中国实验室越来越多地让其 MoE 架构适配国产硬件，这一立场每个月都显得更明智。据报道，DeepSeek 把 V4 的早期访问权给了华为和寒武纪而不是 Nvidia 和 AMD，此后一个华为主导的团队在昇腾芯片上对 V4-Pro 进行了后训练。

如果美国*真的*在 AI 上落后于中国，那些帮助加速了这一中国替代 AI 硬件生态诞生的美国对华鹰派，应该承担相当大一部分责任。

原文：Something that merits more discussion amongst those who believe it was a mistake by both the Biden and Trump administrations to allow the export of less powerful GPUs to China:

Since the summer of 2025, it has increasingly been *China*, not just DC, blocking Nvidia GPUs in China. Within weeks of Secretary Lutnick saying the goal was to get Chinese developers "addicted" to the American technology stack, Beijing began pressuring its tech giants to stop buying the H20. Then it ordered them to stop buying the RTX Pro 6000D, sent customs teams to the ports to crack down on Nvidia chip imports, and barred foreign AI chips from new state-funded data centers. And as of last week, Reuters reports that roughly 600,000 H200s are sitting in Hong Kong because Beijing won't approve large-scale imports of a chip that is still better than anything China's own industry makes at scale.

If allowing the export of these less powerful GPUs was a policy mistake that *advantaged* China, then why is *China* the one blocking them?

From my perspective, China understands that if they are dependent on American AI hardware - either on the ground in China or accessed remotely via the wide range of southeast Asian clouds (which include SE Asian availability zones from the largest American hyperscalers) - then we have immense leverage over them in any future conflict. Being able to turn off the clouds instantly is actually the greatest leverage.

Yes, Chinese AI labs still want Nvidia chips. Gray market prices for H200 servers have roughly tripled over the past year. But that is exactly the point. Beijing is willing to impose real costs on its own AI labs to avoid dependence on American GPUs, which tells you how Beijing views GPU imports: not as a gift, but as a threat to its own ambitions.

Maybe, just maybe, selling China GPUs that trail our own state of the art, so that Chinese developers stay “addicted” to American GPUs and China doesn't build an alternative AI hardware ecosystem that we have no control or influence over *and* that China could share over time with our adversaries, was actually a smart, pro-American national defense policy. And letting them access southeast Asian clouds might be an even smarter policy as so easy to turn clouds off.

I believed and still believed it was smart policy. And I say this as someone whose posts here are hawkish enough on China that I won't travel there anymore.

And note that both the Biden and Trump administration allowed the export of less powerful GPUs to China for all of these reasons. It was only under the Trump administration that China woke up to the risk of what was actually a pretty smart, pro-national defense policy and then China began restricting GPUs.

The lesson of the H20 is that a deliberately crippled chip doesn't keep China on our stack. Beijing moved against the H20 and RTX Pro 6000D as it concluded that domestic Chinese chips had caught up to them. So if China develops an alternative AI hardware ecosystem that it shares with Russia, Iran and North Korea, we will look back and think it would have been even smarter to offer China the B30A in 2025 instead of the H200s we eventually approved case-by-case. Especially given the H200 is not the frontier: with Vera Rubin now in full production, it is two generations behind what American labs are buying.

All of this is to say that Jensen has been consistently lobbying for a position that was adapted by both the Biden *and* Trump admins *and* looks smarter every month that goes by as Chinese labs increasingly adapt their MoE architectures to domestic Chinese hardware. DeepSeek reportedly gave Huawei and Cambricon early access to V4 instead of Nvidia and AMD, and a Huawei-led team has since post-trained V4-Pro on Ascend chips.

And if America *ever* falls behind China in AI, the China hawks here in America who helped accelerate the creation of this alternative Chinese AI hardware ecosystem will deserve a significant share of the blame.

对的部分：“这导致了一种怪异的局面：两国政府中不同的官员群体同时把向中国出售 H200 视为一种需要争取和/或防范的‘让步’。”

完全错误的部分：“尽管可能并非有意，中国意外地实现了两全其美：一个受保护、免受美国出口竞争的封闭硬件市场，以及一个被悄然赋权、可以租用或走私任何所需资源去追赶美国前沿的软件产业。”

如果中国的软件产业（即 AI 实验室）“被悄然赋权、可以租用或走私任何所需资源去追赶美国前沿”，那么这个“封闭硬件市场”在功能上就毫无用处。

没有实验室积极使用，就无法开发出有竞争力的硬件。美国投资者评估加速器初创公司时把这称为“冷启动”问题。如果实验室不用，AI 硬件就无法演进、无法变得有竞争力。

这正是中国限制获取 Nvidia GPU 的原因。

原文：Yes: “This has led to a bizarre situation where different sets of officials across both governments simultaneously view the sale of H200s to China as a “concession” to be fought for and/or guarded against.:”

Utterly wrong: “Though it probably did not mean to, China has managed to accidentally achieve the best of both worlds: a captive hardware market protected from competitive U.S. exports, and a software industry quietly empowered to rent or smuggle whatever resources it needs to chase the American frontier.”

The “captive hardware market” is functionally useless if the Chinese software industry, i.e. AI labs, are “quietly empowered to rent or smuggle whatever resources it needs to chase the American frontier.”

You cannot develop competitive hardware without the labs actively using it. Investors call this the “cold start” problem when evaluating accelerator startups here in America. AI hardware cannot evolve and become competitive if labs don’t use it.

That is why China restricts access to Nvidia GPUs.

2026-11-09 18:05 北京时间 ｜ Source: @nodiligence ｜ 原帖链接

对于 AI 实验室来说，燃气发电机相比它所驱动的芯片太便宜了，因此为了提前拿到一台，它愿意付出任何代价。

Anthropic 花 34 亿美元买下 1.7GW 的 Caterpillar 发电机——这些是圣安东尼奥电力开发商 OnePWR 两年前订购却无力付款的。含安装约合每 kW 2000 美元，而它买到的其实是排队的位置。

这些电力对他们的价值：
——一个 72 GPU 的 GB300 机架峰值 155kW，算上冷却约每颗 GPU 2.5kW。1.7GW 大约可以运行 68 万颗
——按 GB300 五年合约约 4 美元/小时计，相当于每月约 20 亿美元的算力
——所以这些发电机的成本约等于它们所驱动算力七周的收入

（我还不知道它们在哪里运行，也不知道是主供电还是电网接入后的备用。如果是备用，它们能让一个站点比电力公司线路就绪提前几个月上线，而那几个月每个月同样价值 20 亿美元。）

所以排队价格只会往一个方向走。一个有每月 20 亿美元理由插队的实验室，会出价高于任何需要以同一台发电机做抵押借款的 neocloud，因为 neocloud 的贷方按燃气电厂来估值它，而不是按七周的 GPU 租金。

Caterpillar 还要多久会开始按 GPU 租金、而不是按制造成本给发电机定价？

原文：for an AI lab, a gas generator is so cheap next to the chips it switches on that it'll pay whatever it takes to get one early.

anthropic paid $3.4bn for 1.7GW of caterpillar generators that onepwr, a san antonio power developer, ordered two years ago and couldn't fund. that's about $2,000 per kW with the install, and what it bought was the place in the queue.

what that power is worth to them:
-- a 72 GPU GB300 rack peaks at 155kW, so with cooling call it 2.5kW a GPU. 1.7GW runs about 680,000 of them
-- at the ~$4 an hour a GB300 goes for on a five year contract, that's about $2bn of compute a month
-- so the generators cost about seven weeks of what they switch on

(i don't know yet where they run or whether they're the main supply or backup behind a grid hookup. if it's backup, they're what lets a site switch on months before the utility's line is ready, and those months are worth the same $2bn each.)

so the queue price only goes one way. a lab with a $2bn a month reason to skip the line will outbid any neocloud that has to borrow against the same generator, because the neocloud's lender values it like a gas plant, not like seven weeks of GPU rent.

how long before caterpillar starts pricing its generators off the GPU rent instead of what they cost to build?

2026-11-09 20:16 北京时间 ｜ Source: @nodiligence ｜ 原帖链接

$AVGO $AMD $MSFT $ORCL $SPCX $CRWV $META $GOOGL

民主党拿下国会两天后，SpaceX 和 CoreWeave 的 CDS 创下纪录，而彭博报道大型 hyperscaler 的保护成本反而下降了。所以市场已经在区分为 AI 建设买单的公司和为其提供融资的公司。

看看现在谁在承担风险，这有点道理：
——Broadcom 为 Anthropic 的芯片融资提供了约 720 亿美元的兜底，
——AMD 对合作伙伴数据中心的担保在一个季度内从 41 亿美元增至 77 亿美元。

路透社引用 UBS 估计，美国计划在 2028 年投运的数据中心容量中约 30% 可能被暂停。

如果发生这种情况，我认为 Microsoft 或 Meta 只是少花点钱，但为租约提供担保或以建设项目做抵押借款的一方仍然要承担责任；而且 12 月加息概率回到 75% 以上，这些钱短期内不会变便宜。

从谁还能借到钱也能看出来：
——Meta 上周发行了 150 亿美元美元债和 90 亿欧元欧元债，抛售当天欧元债仍高于面值，而
——Oracle 完成了 200 亿美元发行，但债券利差比 2 月份多付了最多 175 个基点。

奇怪的是，给这些风险定价的市场有多小。IG 指数中十个 AI 名字的净 CDS 约 290 亿美元，对应约 8000 亿美元债务，所以我认为几家银行和基金同时对冲就能大幅推动价格，我一直在想这些纪录中有多少是真实恐慌，又有多少只是大家同时对冲。

原文：$AVGO $AMD $MSFT $ORCL $SPCX $CRWV $META $GOOGL

two days after democrats took congress, spacex and coreweave CDS hit records, and bloomberg reported that protection on the big hyperscalers actually got cheaper. so the market is already pulling apart the companies paying for the AI build from the ones that financed it.

which kind of makes sense when you look at who is holding the risk now.

-- broadcom has about $72bn of backstops on anthropic's chip financing,
-- AMD's guarantees on partner data centres went from $4.1bn to $7.7bn in one quarter.

reuters quoted a UBS estimate that about 30% of the US data centre capacity planned for 2028 could get paused.

if that happens, i think microsoft or meta just spend less, but whoever guaranteed the leases or borrowed against the build is still on the hook, and with december hike odds back above 75% that money isn't getting cheaper any time soon.

you can see it in who can still borrow too.
-- meta sold $15bn of dollar bonds and €9bn of euro bonds last week and the euro bonds were above par on the day of the sell-off, while
-- oracle got its $20bn done but paid up to 175bp more on its bonds than it did in february.

the strange part is how small the market setting these prices is. there's about $29bn of net CDS on the ten AI names in the IG index against roughly $800bn of debt, so i think a few banks and funds hedging at the same time can push it around a lot, and i keep wondering how much of these records is real fear and how much is just everyone hedging at once.

2026-11-09 21:34 北京时间 ｜ Source: @nodiligence ｜ 原帖链接

$GOOGL

开发商最好的资产是一块有电的场地，而 Google 刚刚表明，它宁愿早点把场地拿过来，也不愿意付给开发商租金。

Crusoe 在怀俄明州夏延附近的园区上花了一年多：土地、许可、与隔壁 Tallgrass 的供电方案。Google 本应是租户。它不喜欢成本和进度，6 月把 Crusoe 挤出局，现在拥有该场地并计划投入 170 亿美元建设。

所以 Crusoe 拿到了一次性的前期工作报酬，失去了它指望的多年租金（Google 支付的价格未披露）。

我认为每个只有一个超大租户的开发商都面临同样的上限，因为租户的借款成本比开发商低，一旦开发商在成本或工期上出现差池，买下场地就比等待更划算。

Crusoe 在 9 月以 309 亿美元估值融资 39 亿美元，那时 Google 已经离开。下一个考验是它为 Microsoft 在阿比林建设的 900MW 园区，首栋建筑预计 2027 年中交付。

如果那个项目延误，Microsoft 会不会像 Google 那样做？

原文：$GOOGL

a developer's best asset is a site with power on it, and google just showed it'll take the site sooner than pay the developer rent on it.

crusoe spent more than a year on the campus near cheyenne, wyoming: the land, the permits, a power plan with tallgrass next door. google was supposed to be the tenant. it didn't like the cost or the schedule, pushed crusoe out in june, and now owns the site and plans to spend $17bn building it.

so crusoe gets paid once for the groundwork and loses the years of rent it was counting on (the price google paid wasn't disclosed).

i think every developer with one giant tenant is capped the same way, because the tenant borrows cheaper than the developer does, so the moment the developer slips on cost or timing, buying the site beats waiting for it.

crusoe raised $3.9bn at a $30.9bn value in september, after google had already walked. the next test is the 900MW campus it's building for microsoft in abilene, first building due mid 2027.

if that one misses, does microsoft do what google did?

2026-11-10 00:02 北京时间 ｜ Source: @nodiligence ｜ 原帖链接

$NVDA $QCOM $FJTSY $ARM $AMZN $SKM

Nvidia 已经不再试图把机架里的每一颗芯片都卖给你，而是确保机架里的每一颗芯片都通过 Nvidia 来通信。

Rebellions 与 Nvidia 和 SK Telecom 签约，通过 NVLink Fusion（Nvidia 的芯片间互连，让 72 颗芯片像一颗大芯片一样工作）把其 R200（一款韩国推理芯片）接入 Nvidia GPU。他们将测试的方案是：Nvidia GPU 负责 prefill（读取整个提示，重数学运算），R200 负责 decode（逐 token 写出回答，主要在等待内存）。R200 要到 2028 年才推出。

Rebellions 并不是第一个接入这条链路的外部芯片：
——高通和富士通把服务器 CPU 接入它
——Arm 把它内置进授权给所有人的 CPU 设计
——亚马逊正在设计其自研 AI 芯片 Trainium4 以接入它
——Rebellions 现在承担 decode 那一半工作

而 Nvidia 自己今年对 decode 的解决方案是把 Rubin GPU 与 Groq LPX 机架配对，而不是一颗更好的 GPU。

我认为 Nvidia 已经接受，decode——每当 agent 多写一点就会增长的那一半——将流向不是 GPU 的更便宜芯片。拥有这根“线”，就是它在自己没有计算的 token 上仍然能收钱的方式。

所以当 R200 在 2028 年出现时，SK Telecom 最终是按 token 付给 Nvidia 更少的钱，还是为更少的 GPU 和一条无法替换的链路付同样的钱？

原文：$NVDA $QCOM $FJTSY $ARM $AMZN $SKM

nvidia has stopped trying to sell you every chip in the rack. it's making sure every chip in the rack talks through nvidia instead.

rebellions signed with nvidia and SK telecom to wire its R200 (a korean inference chip) into nvidia GPUs over NVLink fusion (nvidia's chip-to-chip link, the thing that lets 72 chips act like one big one). the plan they're going to test: the nvidia GPU does prefill (reading the whole prompt, heavy maths) and the R200 does decode (writing the answer one token at a time, which mostly sits waiting on memory). the R200 isn't due until 2028.

rebellions isn't the first outside chip on that link:
-- qualcomm and fujitsu connect their server CPUs to it
-- arm built it into the CPU designs it licenses to everyone else
-- amazon is designing trainium4, its own AI chip, to sit on it
-- rebellions now takes the decode half of the job

and nvidia's own fix for decode this year was pairing rubin GPUs with groq LPX racks, not a better GPU.

i think nvidia has accepted that decode, the half that grows every time an agent writes more, is going to cheaper chips that aren't GPUs. owning the wire is how it still gets paid on a token it didn't compute.

so when the R200 shows up in 2028, does SK telecom end up paying nvidia less per token, or the same money for fewer GPUs and a link it can't swap out?

2026-11-10 04:30 北京时间 ｜ Source: @flyingwhale919 ｜ 原帖链接

投行伯恩斯坦认为，这场政治风暴对AI基建的实际影响，可能没有股价反应所暗示的那么严重。

该行表示，尽管选举结果增加了部分州数据中心项目的监管风险，但考虑到既有项目的建设进度、政策落地难度以及数据中心对当地经济的贡献，市场可能高估了本轮政策变化的冲击。

该行估计，在更现实的情境下，美国全国可信数据中心项目储备受到的影响可能仅为2%至4%。

根据伯恩斯坦统计，美国处于反对或限制数据中心建设政策环境中的项目储备，在选举前后由249GW增加至306GW。

不过，这些项目并非全部会实际建成。按照伯恩斯坦对项目可实现性的评估，相关「可信容量」由78GW增加至105GW，新增约27GW。更重要的是，在新增的27GW中，约10GW已经开工，并受到既有项目豁免安排保护，真正可能面临更严格监管的容量约为17GW。
伯恩斯坦测算，即使这17GW最终全部无法落地，对全国可信项目储备的冲击也约为9%。而在更可能出现的情境下，只有部分项目被取消或延后，实际影响可能缩小至2%至4%。

引用原帖（@flyingwhale919）：美股AI基建板块在上周经历了灾难性的一周，许多人归咎于民主党中选胜出带来的冲击，并担心AI数据中心建设狂潮就此转向。我对此倒不担心，详见：https://t.co/G31ESTBP5A

—美国中选容易让人产生一种错觉：共和党支持AI基建，民主党反对AI基建。因此中选结果出来，民主党大胜必然意味着AI基建的衰落。但事情没有那么简单，实际情况是：越来越多的选民反对AI基建，因此共和党也不得不调整了策略，也开始强调加强AI监管。因此，我认为AI基建本身就面临阶段性逆风(AI监管审批的加强，是两党共识），只是美国中选成为了一个引爆点。

—在最新选举结果出来后，虽然民主党仍然有议员提出永久禁止AI的提案，但是更多胜选的民主党候任州长已经展现出灵活务实的一面，表态说要在保护民众的利益这个前提下，继续推进AI建设（美国版的“两手抓，两手都要硬”）。

美股AI基建板块在上周经历了灾难性的一周，许多人归咎于民主党中选胜出带来的冲击，并担心AI数据中心建设狂潮就此转向。我对此倒不担心，详见：https://t.co/G31ESTBP5A

—美国中选容易让人产生一种错觉：共和党支持AI基建，民主党反对AI基建。因此中选结果出来，民主党大胜必然意味着AI基建的衰落。但事情没有那么简单，实际情况是：越来越多的选民反对AI基建，因此共和党也不得不调整了策略，也开始强调加强AI监管。因此，我认为AI基建本身就面临阶段性逆风(AI监管审批的加强，是两党共识），只是美国中选成为了一个引爆点。

—在最新选举结果出来后，虽然民主党仍然有议员提出永久禁止AI的提案，但是更多胜选的民主党候任州长已经展现出灵活务实的一面，表态说要在保护民众的利益这个前提下，继续推进AI建设（美国版的“两手抓，两手都要硬”）。

—已经有几个关键的AI基础设施厂商表态：1）加强与社区民众的沟通 2）承担更多，包括强调企业承担自身电力成本、帮助降低居民电费、增加教师和公共服务人员收益、培训和雇用当地劳动力等。3）考虑多地（包括海外布局）。从这些表态可以看出，厂商这一块会付出更多，包括时间，精力，与金钱，但并没有退缩。

$NVDA $AMD $META $CRWV $MSFT $GOOG $AMZN $NBIS

—对AI基建潮流后继发展的判断：

从产业的角度，AI基建仍然会增长（最核心的原因在于终端需求仍然在增长），但增长速度会放缓（建设过程中面对的困难在增加，原来可能仅是工程这个角度，现在则至少要增加民众支持，监管，人力，财力等多个维度）

从股市的角度，则AI基建板块中期面临的最大问题是估值的下调。股价是由基本面和估值两大因素共同决定的。当基本面向好时，人们很容易给予更高的估值，形成“双击”；但是当基本面遭遇短期挫折的时候，不确定因素的增加，会导致估值同步下挫，形成“双杀”。但是，通常人们的焦点在于基本面，而常常忽略了估值的变化。

2026-11-09 09:38 北京时间 ｜ Source: @jukan05 ｜ 原帖链接

台湾半导体分析师陆行之（Andrew Lu）对黄仁勋近期丑闻的看法：

美国为何借丑闻拔剑：三大战略目标

这桩丑闻的目的绝不是揭露婚外情，而是要强行推进三项关键目标——由于黄仁勋的反对，这些目标在中美科技战中一直毫无进展。

第一刀：彻底摧毁黄仁勋在华盛顿反制裁游说的正当性

过去两年，硅谷反对全面禁止对华芯片出口声音最大、最有影响力的企业领袖就是黄仁勋。他一直对白宫说同样的话：“把中国市场让给华为，最后死掉的只会是美国科技产业。”

但一旦美国情报部门把被点名为黄仁勋婚外情对象的女子定性为“为中国国家安全部工作、试图引导美国放松芯片管制的间谍”，他为放松管制所做的每一次游说都具有了完全不同的政治含义。这成了国家安全问题，引发外国间谍利用秘密关系施加影响的可能性。

黄仁勋再也不能在国会或白宫面前为中国市场说一句话。如果他这么做，就会被打上“中了情报圈套、至今仍未清醒”的标签。

第二刀：撤回对华 H200 出口许可，切断中国空壳公司与东南亚走私网络之间的联系

看看两篇报道的时间点。

11 月 8 日，就在黄仁勋丑闻爆出的同一天，《纽约时报》发表了一篇调查报道，记者亲自走访了美国商务部批准经手 Nvidia H200 芯片的数十家中国公司，北京的新矩阵（New Matrix）就是其中之一。据报道，获准购买芯片的公司注册地址原来是空无一人的“幽灵办公室”，部分公司与美国制裁名单上的实体以及与中国军方签约的企业关系密切。

一篇爆料说 Nvidia CEO 与负责芯片走私的新加坡公司 Megaspeed 的女间谍有染；另一篇说商务部批准购买 H200 的中国公司不过是代表中国军方交易的空壳公司。这两篇报道的目的很明确：施压特朗普政府和商务部全面撤销或冻结对华 H200 出口许可，并进一步迫使对“芯片可经由新加坡及东南亚其他地区第三方运营的数据中心转移”这一漏洞进行全面整改。

第三刀：通过国会监督和独立调查，给 Nvidia 套上国家安全的缰绳

作为全球市值最大的公司和 AI 领域的主导力量，Nvidia 此前在经营上享有广泛自主权。但这一事件之后，负责中国、外交和情报事务的三个国会委员会已开始直接审查 Nvidia 的内部安全措施，甚至施压 Nvidia 董事会成立独立调查委员会，调查公司自己的创始人兼 CEO。

实际上，华盛顿的国家安全建制已经在 Nvidia 董事会内部安插了一只看门狗。从现在起，目标是确保一切——直到 Nvidia 每一颗高性能芯片的最终去向——都严格符合美国国家安全战略。这也意味着再也没有空间去打擦边球，或推出专门针对监管门槛定制规格的芯片。

结论

“丑闻是一枚准时引爆的雷管，真正的目标是堵住芯片外流的漏洞。”

黄仁勋犯了在私生活和人际关系上放松警惕的致命错误。最终他落入了美人计——情报战的教科书式手段——并被攻破。美国国家安全和情报机构无情地利用了他私生活暴露的弱点。结果，他们一举解决了三个棘手问题：Nvidia 在白宫游说放松对华限制、H200 芯片流入中国空壳公司，以及经由新加坡的走私。

定于 11 月 18 日举行的国会闭门听证会，将是美国认真着手进一步收紧对华 AI 算力制裁的决定性转折点。

原文：Taiwanese semiconductor analyst Andrew Lu’s take on the recent scandal involving Jensen Huang:

Why the US Seized on the Scandal to Draw Its Sword: Three Strategic Objectives

The purpose of this scandal is by no means to expose an affair. It is to force through three critical objectives that had made no headway in the tech war between the US and China because Jensen Huang opposed them.

The first cut: Completely destroy the legitimacy of Jensen Huang's lobbying against sanctions in Washington

Over the past two years, the loudest and most influential business leader in Silicon Valley opposing a total ban on chip exports to China has been Jensen Huang. He has told the White House the same thing all along: "Cede the Chinese market to Huawei, and the only one that ends up dead is the American tech industry."

But the moment US intelligence labels the woman named as Huang's affair partner "a spy working for China's Ministry of State Security who sought to steer the US toward easing its chip controls," every bit of lobbying he has done for looser controls takes on an entirely different political meaning. It becomes a national security matter, raising the possibility that a foreign spy used a secret relationship to exert influence.

Huang can no longer say a single word in defense of the Chinese market before Congress or the White House. If he does, he will be branded as someone who "fell for an intelligence operation and still hasn't come to his senses."

The second cut: Reverse the H200 export approvals for China and sever the link between Chinese shell companies and Southeast Asian smuggling networks

Look at the timing of the two reports.

On November 8, the very day the Jensen Huang scandal broke, The New York Times published an investigation for which its reporters went in person to dozens of Chinese companies that the US Commerce Department had approved to handle Nvidia's H200 chips. New Matrix in Beijing was one of them. According to the report, the registered addresses of companies approved to buy the chips turned out to be empty "ghost offices," and some of the companies had close ties to entities on US sanctions lists and to firms under contract with the Chinese military.

One exposé says Nvidia's CEO was having an affair with a female spy from Megaspeed, the Singapore company that handled the chip smuggling. The other says the Chinese companies Commerce approved to buy H200s are nothing but shell companies trading on behalf of the Chinese military. The aim of these two reports is clear. They are meant to pressure the Trump administration and the Commerce Department into fully revoking or freezing H200 export approvals for China and, going further, to force a sweeping overhaul of the loophole that let chips be diverted through data centers run by third parties in Singapore and elsewhere in Southeast Asia.

The third cut: Put Nvidia on a national security leash through congressional oversight and an independent investigation

As the world's largest company by market cap and the dominant force in AI, Nvidia has until now enjoyed broad autonomy in how it runs its business. But in the wake of this incident, the three congressional committees responsible for China, foreign affairs, and intelligence have begun examining Nvidia's internal security measures directly. They have even pressured Nvidia's board into forming an independent investigation committee to look into the company's own founder and CEO.

In effect, Washington's national security establishment has planted a watchdog right inside Nvidia's board. From now on, the goal is to make sure that everything, down to where each and every one of Nvidia's high performance chips ends up, conforms strictly to US national security strategy. It also means there is no more room for skirting the edge of the rules or releasing chips with specs specially tailored to regulatory thresholds.

Conclusion

"The scandal was a detonator set off right on cue. The real goal was to plug the holes chips were leaking through."

Jensen Huang made the fatal mistake of letting his guard down in his private life and personal relationships. In the end, he fell into a honey trap, the textbook tactic of intelligence warfare, and was compromised. America's national security and intelligence agencies ruthlessly exploited the weakness his private life had exposed. As a result, they solved three thorny problems in a single stroke: Nvidia's lobbying at the White House to ease restrictions on China, the flow of H200 chips into Chinese shell companies, and smuggling routed through Singapore.

The closed congressional hearing set for November 18 will be the decisive turning point at which the US moves in earnest to tighten its sanctions on China's AI compute even further.

9 日确认，三星电子半导体蚀刻工艺配方（recipe）被泄露给了美国设备商 Lam Research 的韩国业务组织。

Lam Research 向三星电子半导体产线供应蚀刻和沉积设备。设备运行时产生的数据存储在产线上安装的监控服务器等处。

为改进设备性能和维护，Lam Research 可在与三星电子约定的范围内获取部分数据。其韩国组织 Lam Research Korea 向三星电子申请并获取这些数据。

在此过程中，三星电子未允许导出的工艺配方信息以单独文件形式流向了 Lam Research Korea。三星电子于今年下半年察觉此事。调查结果显示，由于 Lam Research Korea 方面的不当请求，超出许可范围的信息导出曾多次发生。

原文：삼성전자 반도체 식각 공정 레시피가 미국 장비업체 램리서치의 한국 사업 조직에 유출된 것으로 9일 확인됐다.

램리서치는 삼성전자 반도체 생산라인에 식각과 증착 장비 등을 공급한다. 장비를 운용하면서 발생하는 데이터는 생산라인에 설치된 모니터링 서버 등에 저장된다.

램리서치는 장비 성능 개선과 유지보수 등을 위해 삼성전자와 합의한 범위 안에서 일부 데이터를 받을 수 있었다. 한국 조직인 램리서치코리아가 삼성전자에 요청해 이 데이터를 받아 왔다.

이 과정에서 삼성전자가 반출을 허용하지 않은 공정 레시피 정보가 별도 파일로 램리서치코리아에 넘어간 것으로 확인됐다. 삼성전자는 올해 하반기 이 사안을 인지했다. 조사를 벌인 결과 램리서치코리아 측 부정 요청으로 허용 범위를 벗어난 정보 반출이 수차례 반복됐던 것으로 전해졌다.

据媒体报道，台积电联席 COO 侯永清说，客户经常问：“既然你们同时在建 20 座厂，能不能建 30 座？”

答案是不能，因为由于劳动力短缺及各工地的其他问题，行业目前还无法承受那样的规模。

在其主要 2nm 厂区——高雄 Fab 22（第四期）和宝山——发生事故后，台积电正在加强监督。在 11 月 17 日的供应链论坛上，公司预计将宣布新措施，可能包括成立专门的施工安全工作组，以及对未能改进的企业取消订单。$TSM

原文：TSMC Co-COO Cliff Hou says clients frequently ask: “Since you’re building 20 plants at once, could you do 30?”

The answer is no, media report, because the industry can’t handle that scale yet due to labor shortages and other issues across construction sites.

TSMC is tightening oversight after accidents at its primary 2nm fab sites — Fab 22 (Phase 4) in Kaohsiung and in Baoshan. 原文：Japan's Mitsui Chemicals will buy chip-materials supplier JSR from state-backed Japan Investment Corp. for ¥1.84 trillion yen (US$11.6 billion), with the Development Bank of Japan investing ¥200 billion, Mitsui said. JSR makes photoresists, used to pattern circuits onto wafers.

美国数据中心建设的最大风险已经在一个州生效。

“最大的单一风险不是某个候选人，因为在许多情况下两党及候选人都希望开发商接受更严格的条件。”

“以德州为例，冻结已经存在。德州在 9 月底停止向数据中心项目发放 TCEQ 许可，此外 ERCOT 从 8 月起开始进行并网审计。”

“仅德州就占 2028 年美国新增容量的近 30%。所以仅这一个州、这一个市场损失几个月时间，就可能改变全国数字。”

原文：The biggest risk to the US data center buildout is already in effect in one state.

“The single biggest risk isn’t a single candidate, because in many cases both parties and candidates want developers to accept stricter terms.”

“In the case of Texas, it’s a freeze that already exists. Texas stopped issuing TCEQ permits to data center projects at the end of September, on top of an ERCOT interconnection audit that started in August.”

“Texas alone is close to 30% of US additions in 2028. So a few months of lost time in that one state, that one market, could move the national number.”

2026-11-10 00:00 北京时间 ｜ Source: @SemiAnalysis_ ｜ 原帖链接

在我们建模的 14nm 情景中，CXMT 的首个 4F² 单元将比其现有 G5 单元大约 10%。不过，cell-on-periphery 键合仍可能使整体 die 密度相比 G5 提升约 10%，其中大部分收益来自把外围电路移到单独的晶圆上。（3/6）

原文：In the 14nm scenario we modeled, CXMT’s first 4F² cell would be ~10% larger than its existing G5 cell. Cell-on-periphery bonding could nevertheless improve overall die density by ~10% vs. that of G5, with most of that gain coming from moving the peripheral circuitry onto a separate wafer. (3/6)

所回复原帖（@SemiAnalysis_）：As we discussed in our October 16 note, the economics depend on the actual dimensions and manufacturing process behind the 4F² label. (2/6) 链接

2026-11-10 00:00 北京时间 ｜ Source: @SemiAnalysis_ ｜ 原帖链接

密度提升伴随着额外成本。必须加工两片晶圆，最终良率取决于存储单元晶圆良率、外围晶圆良率和键合良率。这些权衡决定了该设计能否带来更低的每比特成本。（4/6）

原文：That density improvement comes with additional costs. Two wafers must be processed, and final yield depends on the cell-wafer yield, peripheral-wafer yield and bonding yield. Those trade-offs determine whether the design delivers a lower cost per bit. (4/6)

[From:] Jensen Huang

Subj.: A message to our company

Nov. 8, 2026

To my colleagues at NVIDIA,

I owe my family and all of you an apology.

I was unfaithful to my wife. My actions hurt her and our children. There is no excuse for what I did. I am deeply sorry.

Throughout my life, my family has given me love and support. They deserve my honesty and loyalty. Instead, I let them down and damaged the life we built together. I take full responsibility for what I did and the hurt I caused, and I accept the criticism that comes with it.

I love our country and our company. I am committed to protecting NVIDIA and supporting America’s national security. I am proud and grateful to be an American.

My family bears no responsibility for my choices. I do. Please give them privacy.

— Jensen

Statement from Nvidia’s board of directors:

The NVIDIA Board is initiating an independent investigation in light of recent reporting that founder and CEO Jensen Huang has been the target of a foreign intelligence effort.

A committee of independent directors, assisted by outside independent counsel, will oversee the investigation.

The Board prioritizes U.S. national and economic security, which is paramount to the interests of NVIDIA and its shareholders.

While the Board has no reason to believe that either national security or NVIDIA secrets were compromised, the Board’s inquiry will examine all available information.

Jensen fully supports the review and has committed to cooperating with it.

The Board has confidence in Jensen and his leadership team and will provide further updates as appropriate.

"

Anthropic 今天发布了新的头号模型，但它自己的发布文章却告诉你不要先用它？？

对于大多数编程和知识工作，他们推荐从 Opus 5.5 开始，称其为“我们最强的日常主力”。

我想知道为什么

1. 先看日常 agent 工作。

——在 OSWorld（模型驱动真实桌面、点击各种应用完成任务）上，Fable 得 89%，Opus 87%。

——在维护者真正会合并的代码上，高 effort 的 Fable 与 Opus 的最佳成绩持平，而每个任务成本约为一半

Artificial Analysis 的独立数据与此一致。终端编程：两者都是 60%。办公任务：在误差范围内。

在那里，最大 effort 下一个 Fable 任务花费 26.5 美元，Opus 10 美元。对于大致相同的短任务，你要付约 2.6 倍的钱

那如果给它一个巨大的任务呢？

Anthropic 让 1000 个 Fable agent 形式化费马大定理，即把整个证明改写成计算机可以逐行检查的语言。

Fable 5.2 用 30 小时完成。
同样设置下，Opus 停在 27%

在 Remote Labor Index（模仿真实自由职业工作的项目）上也是同样模式：Fable 41%，Opus 21%。

还有一位工程师让它运行 1000 多个子 agent 连续八天，构建了一个能在笔记本电脑上启动的操作系统

我的解读：对于点击应用或修 bug 之类的短任务，Opus 5.5 基本就够了。最大 effort 的 Fable 为 2 个基准点花的钱太多了。

当一个任务要运行数小时且必须保持方向时，或者作为把简单部分分派给更便宜模型的“老板”时，Fable 5.2 物有所值

据 IFR 报道 Anthropic 周二开始 IPO 推介，他们带着入场的旗舰模型，却是一个告诉大多数人留给大任务用的模型？

很想看到独立的长周期测试，看能否复现费马和自由职业工作上的差距

原文：Anthropic shipped its new #1 model today, but its own launch post tells you not to start with it??

for most coding and knowledge work, the recomment starting with opus 5.5, "our strongest daily driver".

i wanted to know why

1. Everyday agent work first.

- on osworld, where the model drives a real desktop and clicks through apps to finish a task, fable gets 89% and opus 87%.

- on code a maintainer would actually merge, fable at high effort ties opus's best for about half the cost per task

the independent numbers from artificial analysis line up. coding in a terminal: both 60%. Office tasks: within the error bars.

At max effort a fable task costs $26.5 there, opus $10. you pay about 2.6x for roughly the same short job

then what if you give it something huge?

Anthropic had 1,000 fable agents formalize fermat's last theorem, meaning rewrite the whole proof in a language a computer can check line by line.

Fable 5.2 finished in 30 hours.
Opus, same setup, stalled at 27%

same pattern on the remote labor index, projects modeled on real freelance work: 41% for Fable, 21% for Opus.

And one engineer had it run 1,000+ subagents for eight days. it built an operating system that boots on a laptop

my read: for short jobs like clicking through an app or fixing a bug, opus 5.5 is basically there. Max-effort fable is a LOT of money for 2 benchmark points.

Fable 5.2 earns it when a job runs for hours and has to stay on track, or as the boss handing easy parts to cheaper models

As IFR reports Anthropic starts marketing its IPO tuesday, the flagship they walk in with is one it tells most people to save for the big jobs?

curious to see independent long horizon tests that don't reproduce the fermat and freelance work gaps

有件事我想说。

我的专长是半导体技术和半导体产业。我有半导体工程博士学位，在硅谷做设计工程师。我还运营着一个有数百名半导体专业人士的社群。通过研讨会和与业内人士的交流，我持续研究技术和产业的走向。

我今年早些时候开始写的文章帮助了许多读者，我的 X 和 Substack 获得了超出预期的关注。我一直心存感激。

作为个人投资者，我有自己的观点，也会分享对某些公司和行业的看法。但分析一家公司的技术和竞争力，与准确预测股价或确切知道何时买卖是两回事。我的专长在前者，不能自称擅长后者。如果我擅长后者，我大概会在纽约华尔街工作，而不是在硅谷。

这就是为什么我分享投资观点时总会注明 NFA 和 DYOR。我的判断有局限，你应该审视其中的推理并自己做决定。事实上，当有人以绝对确定的口吻鲁莽地叫人买卖时，我坚信那个人是骗子。

我看待存储产业也是如此。

我对存储业务仍然乐观。基于我的所见所闻、在行业工作的经验以及技术理解，我仍然在需求趋势和存储技术的结构性变化中看到很多乐观理由。因此，我不会仅仅因为存储股下跌就改变对行业的看法。如果你批评我连抛售都预测不到还谈存储业务，我没什么可回应的。抱歉，那确实不是我擅长的……

如果供给、需求或技术竞争力的变化推翻了我观点的基础，我会改变看法。NVHBM 是我现在最密切关注的。我计划在年底前与存储专家多做交流并分享见解。

所以回到问题，如果你问我：“存储股都跌成这样了，你还看好存储？”我的回答是肯定的，因为我所看到的行业基本面仍然支持这一观点。我不会假装知道股价何时反弹。与此同时，我会继续研究存储业务，检验我观点的基础是否依然成立。

今后，我不会因为某家公司赞助我就夸它，也不会因为持有某公司股票就只写对它有利的文章。我也不会仅仅因为某公司股价涨跌就改变分析的基础。

我想在这里提供的是证据和视角，帮助你理解半导体技术和产业的变化，从而做出自己的判断。你不必同意我的结论。但如果我的文字让技术更容易理解一点、让你对某家公司或行业多一个问题、或帮助你用更具体的证据重新评估投资决策，那么我认为我的研究就尽到了职责。

谢谢。

原文：There is one thing I would like to say.

My expertise is in semiconductor technology and the semiconductor industry. I have a PhD in semiconductor engineering and work as a design engineer in Silicon Valley. I also run a community with hundreds of semiconductor professionals. Through seminars and conversations with people in the industry, I continue to study and research where the technology and the industry are heading.

The articles I started writing earlier this year have helped many readers, and my X and Substack have attracted more attention than I expected. I am always grateful for that.

I have my own views as an individual investor, and I share opinions on certain companies and industries. However, analyzing a company’s technology and competitiveness is different from accurately predicting stock prices or knowing exactly when to buy and sell. My expertise lies in the former. I cannot claim to be good at the latter. If I were, I would probably be working on Wall Street in New York rather than in Silicon Valley.

That is why I always mention NFA and DYOR when sharing my investment views. My judgment has limitations, and you should examine the reasoning and make your own decisions. In fact, when someone recklessly tells people to buy or sell with absolute certainty, I firmly believe that person is a fraud.

The same applies to how I view the memory industry.

I remain optimistic about the memory business. Based on what I see and hear, my experience working in the industry, and my technical understanding, I still see plenty of reasons for optimism in demand trends and the structural changes in memory technology. That is why I do not change my view of the industry simply because memory stocks fall. If you criticize me for discussing the memory business when I could not even predict the selloff, I do not have much to say in response. Sorry, but that simply is not what I am good at…

If changes in supply, demand, or technological competitiveness overturn the basis of my view, I will change my opinion. NVHBM is what I am watching most closely right now. I plan to have more conversations with memory experts toward the end of the year and share my insights.

So, coming back to the question, if you ask me, “Memory stocks have fallen this much, and you are still optimistic about memory?” my answer is yes, because the industry fundamentals I see still support that view. I will not pretend to know when the stocks will rebound. In the meantime, I will keep researching the memory business and checking whether the basis for my view still holds.

Going forward, I will not praise a company simply because it sponsors me, nor will I write only favorable articles about a company simply because I own its stock. I will also not change the basis of my analysis merely because a company’s stock price rises or falls.

What I want to offer here is evidence and perspective that help you understand changes in semiconductor technology and the industry, so you can make your own judgments. You do not have to agree with my conclusions. But if my writing makes technology a little easier to understand, gives you another question to ask about a company or industry, or helps you reassess your investment decisions using more concrete evidence, then I believe my research is doing its job.

Thank you.

原文：🔔 The Daily Semi-Cap — November 8, 2026

📊 SMH: $463.96 (+3.62% vs November 5; last market close: November 6, 2026)

• Nvidia’s board launched an independent investigation into reports that Jensen Huang was targeted by a foreign intelligence effort. Independent directors and outside counsel will oversee it. The board expressed confidence in his leadership and said it has no reason to believe national security or company secrets were compromised. This is a governance overhang—not an announced leadership change.

• Anthropic reportedly agreed to pay $3.4B for 1.7GW of gas-fired generators, taking over OnePWR Solutions’ existing Caterpillar equipment orders and associated installation work. The transaction, struck in late September and newly disclosed today, is not an acquisition of OnePWR. Securing equipment slots can shorten the power bottleneck, but does not eliminate permitting, fuel-supply or construction requirements.

• Terafab’s equipment procurement team has approached Japanese semiconductor-tool manufacturers with ordering plans as Musk pushes to accelerate factory development. That moves the project beyond a capacity ambition toward supplier engagement. The distinction for equipment investors: a procurement roadmap is a potential demand signal, not booked revenue, installed capacity or proof that manufacturing yields will meet targets.

• Britain is resisting a blanket data-center slowdown. Growth minister Dan Tomlinson said construction should continue as essential infrastructure, with local investment and jobs required for government support. The existing roadmap anticipates at least 6GW of AI-capable capacity by 2030. The opportunity is geographic redistribution of infrastructure spending—not an escape from power constraints or community opposition.

• Samsung, SK Hynix and Micron are discussing common fab-robot charging standards with SEMI, including eventual integration of charging information with manufacturing execution systems. Shared infrastructure could make mixed robot fleets practical in older fabs where space is scarce and material handling remains manual. Standards and implementation responsibilities are still being developed; this is an automation-enablement initiative, not a disclosed equipment order.

特斯拉德州锂精炼厂最初投入的原料是锂精矿（锂辉石）。那么下一种原料是什么？

2026.10.29 公开的特斯拉专利申请介绍了一种技术：将黑粉（废电池粉末）在 850°C 下焙烧后，不用酸、只用水洗。据称黑粉中 96% 以上的锂会溶于水，剩余固体加酸后，固体中 99.9% 的镍和钴会溶出。

当现在路上跑的电动车报废时，废电池将成为城市矿山。我想总有一天这些废电池会成为精炼厂的第二种原料，这也将对特斯拉汽车和机器人的电池成本结构产生重大影响。🤔

原文：테슬라 텍사스 리튬 정제 공장에 처음 들어간 원료는 리튬 정광(스포듀민)이었습니다. 그럼 다음 원료는 무엇일까요?

★@levie（保留 1 条 / 窗口内原创及回复 1 条）

2026-11-09 08:44 北京时间 ｜ Source: ★@levie ｜ 原帖链接

我们正在从一个 AI 产出大致取决于你与 AI 来回对话速度的时代，走向一个你可以给一组 agent 设定问题、让它们去为你完成任意大量工作的时期。这意味着生产力提升从大约 20–50%，变成没有真正上限的提升。

Agent 集群以及把复杂工作分散给许多并行执行任务的 agent 的能力，在很多领域带来巨大解锁。这将在许多领域带来质的不同结果。

这已经在编程领域得到验证。接下来，网络安全几乎完全是一个“更多 agent 审查更多东西就会带来好得多结果”的问题空间。我们现在在许多科学领域也看到了同样的情况，这几乎肯定会改变生命科学。

但这在任何可以把基于计算机的工作并行化、以更快完成或达到完全不同质量水平的领域都会越来越成立——而这些其实是当今知识工作中最有价值的一些领域。

我们的大脑并不真正适应我们正在走向的新情境：算力成为我们可以随意调节、用来解决任何问题的变量。我们习惯于认为投入一项任务的时间与产出相关，大多数知识工作或科学领域一直如此。这一点正在永远改变。

原文：We’re moving from an era of AI where the output comes roughly at the rate that you can chat back and forth with the AI to work with you, to a period where you can set a problem for a set of agents to go off and do an arbitrarily large amount of work for you. This means productivity gains that are maybe 20-50% to gains that don’t really have an upper limit.

Agent swarms and the ability to fan out complex work across many agents doing tasks in parallel provide a massive unlock in a lot of spaces. This will lead to qualitatively different outcomes in my fields.

This has already been seen across the coding domain. Next, cyber is almost entirely a problem space where more agents reviewing more things will lead to far better outcomes. We’re seeing the same now in many scientific fields, and this almost certainly will change life sciences.

But this will increasingly be true in any field where you can parallelize computer-based work to complete something faster or do something at a totally different quality level. Which is actually are some of the most valuable domains of knowledge work today.

Our brains aren’t really wired for the new scenario we’re heading into where compute becomes the variable that we can flex to solve any problem that we want. We’re used to thinking that the amount of time that goes into a task is correlated to the output that we get from it. And most domains of knowledge work or science have generally always been correlated with this. That’s changing forever.

引用原帖（@gdb）：frontier models, with lots of test time compute, are becoming the engine of scientific discovery 链接

★@EpochAIResearch（保留 3 条 / 窗口内原创及回复 4 条）

2026-11-10 03:06 北京时间 ｜ Source: ★@EpochAIResearch ｜ 原帖链接

我们估计，仅看 FLOP/s 会把 GB300 相对 H100 的推理优势低估 10 倍。标准的 AI 芯片比较遗漏了推理中最重要的大部分因素。

我们推出推理性能指数（Inference Performance Index，IPI），这一新指标在算力之外还考虑了内存带宽和芯片间通信。这些因素决定了一颗芯片在给定输出速度下能同时支持多少用户或 agent。

以 GB300 当量计的分数让你可以直接比较芯片，并估算一个数据中心、乃至全世界的总推理能力。

原文：We estimate that FLOP/s alone understates GB300’s inference advantage over H100 by 10×. The standard AI chip comparison misses most of what matters for inference.

We’re introducing the Inference Performance Index (IPI), a new measure that accounts for memory bandwidth and inter-chip communication alongside compute. These factors determine how many users or agents a chip can support simultaneously at a given output speed.

Scores in GB300 equivalents let you compare chips directly and estimate the total inference capacity of a data center, or even the world.

考虑到近期关于 AI 对 AI 研发贡献的讨论，这一点值得注意，我们希望很快发布更多这方面的工作。

这是基于我们在发布前仅使用自有基准进行的测试。随着更多基准结果出来，ECI 会持续更新，未来可能变化。

【AI新闻/测评】

@teortaxesTex（保留 34 条 / 窗口内原创及回复 146 条）

2026-11-09 09:14 北京时间 ｜ Source: @teortaxesTex ｜ 原帖链接

我对黄氏婚外情（双关）的冷静看法是：国安部（MSS）在这件事上的操作近乎最优，比北京整体做得更好，而且并非事后看来的偶然。以下是我的思路。

中国人根据实际情况校准自己的行动。2024–2025 年是一场试探：在特朗普——一个始终交易导向、热衷交易与讨价还价、最尊重骨气和蛮力的商人——治下，美国在出口管制上愿意让步多少。为此，他们对“解放日”关税进行了强硬报复，拒绝了 H20 和 B30，用商务部的威胁回应 BIS 的 50% 规则，用切断欧洲供应回应安世半导体被没收，最终达成了釜山协议（2025 年 10 月），并放出其 EUV 光源进展的消息（路透社 12 月公开）。随后特朗普带着 Jensen（同情中国、迫切希望在中国市场永久关闭前重返）以及一众工业/金融 CEO 去了北京；习也去了华盛顿；诸如此类。在他们所能期望的最合作的政府治下，中国人实现了最大可能的缓和。

而他们为此得到的，只是每年最多 75 万颗 H200 的许可，每个实体 7.5 万颗（按传闻价格约合 170 亿美元算力），借口是给特朗普多一点关税收入；外加对海外建设或小规模走私的一些宽容。鹰派用一些胡扯的图表把这说成这些芯片具有战略意义，但实际上，这是对中国示好的礼貌拒绝。华为自己就能造出 H200 级别的系统；在集群层面，950DT 可以说更好。这最多相当于他们当前产能几个月的量，不会改变大局，也救不了他们于严重的供给短缺。

到 3 月，我认为北京也清楚了：共和党很可能在中期选举中惨败，因为它为了以色列的地缘政治需要放弃了国内控制；当时我还不清楚，但他们一定对伊朗的韧性和五角大楼的非理性有更好的情报。因此，民主党会回来。而民主党 a）对 AI 狂热的科技右翼（Jensen 是其造王者，特朗普是其庇护者）持负面极化态度；b）有能力合法地协调国际力量对抗“不自由国家”；c）教条式地执着于对中国发展的“这就是毁灭的样子！！”式极端攻击，尽管与 2018 年甚至 2022 年不同，现在的中国非常有能力报复。此外，他们与末日论/EA 社区以及出口管制倡导中的 Dario 一方关系密切。特朗普仍可通过行政令治理，但他必须节约使用这一权力，而且他有许多更大的优先事项。

此外，恢复算力贸易的技术论据也在瓦解，主要是“Nvidia 的可得性能扼杀中国内部研发、让中国人‘上瘾’或依赖，从而受制于未来的限流与施舍、更害怕对抗”这一观点。我过去也这么主张，因为我没有观察到近期的正反馈循环：对任何中国顶级团队来说，离开 Nvidia 的代价都太高了，尽管它稀缺；代价以在一个非常残酷而神经质的市场中停滞数月来衡量。与美国对计划经济的幻想相反，中共似乎不愿意或无力解决这个战略死亡陷阱，单靠补贴是不够的。

这里一个重要因素仍然是 DeepSeek。我仍不确定 2025 年关于 R2 在昇腾上失败的传闻有多可信（从逻辑上说，从来就没有 R2 项目），但 2025 年底到 2026 年初的大部分时间确实用于转型，最终“鲸鱼”为中国生态的结晶提供了核心，构建了默认的华为-TileLang 技术栈（正如梁文锋从一开始就打算做的）。他们为华为的路线图创造了专属需求和反馈，我现在预计这将加速，并带动二线玩家。
我不确定滞留在香港的 H200 能否改变这一切。鉴于 DeepSeek 此前使用 CUDA 如此成功，无限制销售可能会把这一演变推迟一年左右。但它们本来就受限，额外 7.5 万颗合法 H200 对于鲸鱼想做的事来说……根本不够。所以总的来说，是华盛顿让这一切成为必然。

最后，Nvidia 对美国生态的感知重要性被 AI 本身的进步削弱了。OpenAI 可以直接造出 Jalapeno，至少在第一代就能与 Blackwell 竞争；Anthropic 也会设计出很棒的芯片；TPU 非常出色，Meta 和亚马逊在进步。Broadcom 等足以维持供应链完整性。AI 可以写出超人水平的 kernel（梁文锋也提出了这一点），照这个速度 CUDA 护城河将无法持续，除了一些定制的爱好者实验（每个严肃的实验室现在都能为其大规模工作负载构建更好的技术栈）。更重要的是，Rubin 的爬坡比预期慢、问题重重，而且令人担忧地依赖中国供应商。

重要的是要认识到，卢比奥关于黄氏的备忘录早在一年多以前就已起草。事情并不只是“需要一段时间”，而是需要等到行动变得合宜的时候。按照第 47 任总统头两年的轨迹，对黄的行动代价相对较低。到 2026 年中，形势已经很明显：在下一代 Nvidia 硬件广泛可用之前，打压就会到来。

那么，理性的做法是什么？我认为是继续“榨取”Jensen、收集情报，让企业利用海外算力满足当前运营支出，协助走私无法在国内大规模生产的部件以及真正带来质变的系统（目前是 NVL72 Blackwell），并迅速发展国内供应链。

我认为国安部这一局下得很好。可能仍然不够，但我不确定他们还能做得更好多少。

原文：My cold take on the Huangs Affair (pun intended) is that the MSS has played this near-optimally, better than Beijing writ large, and not just accidentally-in-retrospect. Here is my thinking.

The Chinese are calibrating their moves to facts on the ground. 2024-2025 was an investigation into just how much the US is willing to budge on export controls under Trump, who is a consistently transactional merchant, a deal junkie and haggler respecting backbone and brute force above all. To this end they've retaliated harshly to Liberation Day, refused H20s and B30s, responded to BIS 50% with the MOFCOM threat and to Nexperia seizure with cutting off European supply, secured an eventual Busan deal (Oct 2025), and leaked news on their EUV light source progress (made public by Reuters in Dec). Then Trump went to Beijing with Jensen (China-sympathetic and desperately interested in getting back into their market before it closes forever) at his side and industrial/finance CEOs in tow; and Xi went to Washington; and so on and so forth. The Chinese have achieved the maximum rapproachment possible under the most cooperative administration they could hope for.

And all they've got for it was a permit for max 750K H200s per year, 75K per entity (about $17B worth of compute at alleged prices), under the pretext of marginally more tariff bux for Trump; plus some lenience with regard to overseas buildouts or small-scale smuggling. Hawks catastrophised about that with bullshit charts where those chips would be strategically significant, but really, this was a polite rejection of Chinese advances. Huawei can make H200-grade systems themselves; on a cluster level, 950DTs are arguably better. It's worth at most a few months of their current throughput. It wouldn't have moved the needle or rescued then from crippling supply scarcity.

And by March, I think it also became clear to Beijing that the GOP will most likely crushingly lose the midterms, having abandoned domestic control in favor of Israeli geopolitical needs; it wasn't then clear to me, but they must have had better intel about Iranian resilience and Pentagon's irrationality. Thus, Democrats would have come back. And Democrats are a) negatively polarized against the AI-pilled tech right, of which Jensen is the kingmaker and Trump the patron; b) capable of legitimately coordinating international effort against Unfree Countries; c) dogmatically committed to "this is what annihilation looks like!!" turbo-aggression against Chinese development, even though the current China, unlike the China of 2018 or even 2022, is very capable of retaliation. Plus they have rapport with the doomer/EA community and the Dario side of export control advocacy. Trump can still rule by EOs, but he'll have to ration this power, and he has many larger priorities.

Additionally, technical arguments for resuming compute trade have been unraveling, chiefly the idea that availability of Nvidia can strangle internal R&D and keep the Chinese "addicted" or dependent, thus subject to future throttling and treats and more afraid of confrontation. I used to argue this too, because I didn't observe a near-term positive feedback loop: it was too costly for any top-tier Chinese group to switch away from Nvidia, scarce though it is; the cost measured in months of stagnation in a very cutthroat and neurotic market. The CCP, contra American fantasies about planned economy, seemed unwilling or incapable to address this strategic death trap, and mere subsidies wouldn't have sufficed.

A big factor here is, again, DeepSeek. I am still not sure how legit the rumors about the failed R2-on-Ascend in 2025 were (logicallt speaking, there never was the R2 project), but much of late 2025-early 2026 was indeed dedicated to transition, and in the end the Whale has provided a nucleus for crystallization of the Chinese ecosystem, building the default Huawei-TileLang stack (as Wenfeng intended to do from the start). They create captive demand and feedback for Huawei's roadmap, which I now expect to accelerate and pull up second-fiddle players too.
I'm not sure if H200s stranded in Hong Kong could have changed any of it. Given how successfully DeepSeek had been using CUDA until now, it is possible that unrestricted sales would have postponed this evolution for another year or so. But they were restricted anyway, and extra 75K legal H200s are just… not enough for what Whale intends to do. So, on balance, Washington had made this an inevitability.

Finally, Nvidia's perceived importance for the American ecosystem has been undermined by the very progress in AI. OpenAI can just up and make Jalapeno that is at the very least competitive with Blackwell, at Gen 1; Anthropic will also design a great chip; TPUs are awesome, Meta and Amazon are improving. Broadcom et al. are enough to maintain the supply chain integrity. AIs can write superhuman kernels (Wenfeng also makes this point) and at this rate the CUDA moat will not last except for bespoke hobbyist experiments (every serious lab is now capable of building a better stack for their large scale workloads). On top of it all, Rubin ramp is slower than expected, troubled, and worryingly dependent on Chinese suppliers.

And it is important to realize that Rubio's memo on Huangs had been drafted well over a year ago. Things don't just "take a while", they take as long as it takes for the action to become expedient. Under the trajectory of the first two years of 47th, the Huang operation was relatively costless. By mid-2026, the writing was on the wall that the crackdown is coming before the next generation of Nvidia hardware is broadly available.

What, then, was the rational move? I think it was to keep milking Jensen, collecting intel, letting companies utilize overseas compute for current opex, facilitating smuggling of components that can't be produced at scale domestically and systems that actually make some qualitative difference (NVL72 Blackwells right now), and rapidly developing domestic supply chain.

I think the MSS played this very well. It's probably still not enough, but I'm not sure they could have done much better.

2026-11-09 07:10 北京时间 ｜ Source: @teortaxesTex ｜ 原帖链接

我不明白为什么人们对 DSH（DeepSeek Harness）大惊小怪。
154 万 DAU 只是 DeepSeek DAU 的一小部分。如果它目前每天处理 92T token，那只相当于 19 天前 DeepInfer 开源时其单个推理集群处理量的 59%；从 V4.1F 的变慢和我对其机群动态的粗估来看，这还不到其当前吞吐的四分之一，即便不算第三方提供商。而且模型-harness 协同设计的承诺显然足以吸引重度用户，他们消耗的 token 比普通用户多几个数量级。
此外，它本身就是一个适用于任何 LLM 的精巧 harness，一个易于调整的预打包应用。
不过我确实欣赏它完全瓦解了使用各种“第一方”Claude Code/Codex 仿制品和第一代 agent IDE 的理由。幸运眷顾勇者。DSH 会变得更大。事实上，它最终的 1.0 版本很可能成为 agentic 时代默认的开放操作系统。

原文：I don't see why people are freaking out about DSH
1.54M DAU is a sliver of DeepSeek's DAU. If it's currently processing 92T tokens daily, that's just 59% of what their one inference cluster did at the point of DeepInfer open sourcing, 19 days ago; judging by V4.1F's slowdown and my guesstimates of their fleet dynamics, it's less than a quarter of their current throughput, even ignoring third party providers. And the promise of model-harness codesign is obviously enough to lure the power users, who consume OOMs more tokens than the average.
Plus it's just a neat harness for any LLM, a pre-packaged app that is easy to tweak.
I do however appreciate how it's totally wiping out the argument for using various "first party" Claude Code/Codex me-toos and first-generation agent IDEs. Fortune favours the bold, Chinaman. DSH will grow larger. Indeed, its eventual 1.0 release might well become the default open OS for the agentic era.

对于那些还没有走私数百兆瓦算力、或没有拿到华为承诺的人来说确实如此。

原文：&gt; Whoever builds a million-chip cluster of garbage first wins. Waiting to smuggle in Nvidia chips, or even waiting for Huawei to throw them a bone, is a dead end.

True for everyone not having already smuggled hundreds of megawatts worth or secured Huawei's commitment. https://x.com/teortaxesTex/status/2119574347270762984/photo/1 https://twitter.com/sun_hanchi/status/2119464716515119121

2026-11-09 13:21 北京时间 ｜ Source: @teortaxesTex ｜ 原帖链接

> 我认为推理服务的效率意味着在可预见的未来权重是静态的。

我认为这在实质上是错的。主要是，我认为企业级在 agent 上的支出（以及你这里假设的支出）足以支撑专属部署。想想 DeepSeek-superagent，或我听说的网络安全研究，它们一夜之间运行 5 轮、每轮 200–1000 个 agent 的集群。OpenAI 攻克 NS 的规模更大。如果用集群解决的任务具有很高经济价值，那么不需要太多技术进步就能让在这种模式下的训练收回成本，当然我们仍需解决持续学习的固有问题（固化与脆性、遗忘、缺乏良性遗忘、错误泛化等等）。这大概与你关于激励的说法并不矛盾。

当然，这不会自动帮助面向大众服务的 GPT-X/Claude Y，但它们可以靠 .md 维基式记忆凑合。

我也乐观地认为，经过几轮（或几百、几千轮）上述过程之后，与溯因推理相关的技能可以泛化，因为递归压缩的模式本身会被学会。Agentic RL 已经依赖压缩（见 Inkling），所以我们只需把同样的原则扩展到把集群上下文蒸馏为权重更新。

我预计 DeepSeek 已经在朝这个方向思考。

原文：> I think the efficiency of serving implies the staticness of the weights for the forseable future.

I think this is meaningfully wrong. Chiefly, I think that the enterprise level spending on agents (and the spending you assume here) justifies dedicated deployment. Consider DeepSeek-superagent, or the cyber research I've heard about, they run a sequence of 5 swarms of 200-1000 agents overnight. OpenAI's assault on NS was even larger. If tasks solved with swarms are highly economically valuable, it won't take a lot of technical progress to make training pay back in this regime, though of course we'll still need to solve inherent issues of continual learning (hardening and brittleness, forgetting, lack of benign forgetting, misgeneralization, whatever). Probably this doesn't contradict what you say about incentives.

This doesn't automatically help the GPT-X/Claude Y served to the commoners, of course, but they can make do with .md wiki memories.

I am also optimistic that abduction-relevant skills can generalize after a few (or a few hundred, or thousand) rounds of the process described above, as the pattern of recursive compression itself becomes learned. Agentic RL already relies on compaction (see Inkling https://t.co/qgWTNlEUcp here) so all we need is to extend the same principle to the distillation of swarm-contexts into weight updates.

I expect DeepSeek to think in this direction already

引用原帖（@teortaxesTex）：Very interesting digest of Inkling 2S, for me this is the highest-signal part https://t.co/1YtzYhK3DB https://t.co/DY0flWUlVN 链接

所回复原帖（@doomslide）：@teortaxesTex About abduction I want to clarify my view. I believe what the technology does fundamentally is the simulation of text. I believe this deeply. That's what also allows me to believe that in principle it could simulate the entire service sector. The questions I have about it are at 链接

在 OpenAI 关闭意外开放的“Chat + Work 合并体验”之前，我自己测试了一下。

很多人问我“12 月会是什么样”，如果和我测试的差不多，它将这样运作：

——一个统一的 Chat 界面，可以和模型对话，并从已有的所有变体中选择（GPT-6 Sol、Luna、6.1 Sol、Terra 等）。
——选定模型后可以正常聊天，它足够聪明，能判断你的消息是否需要工具。
——如果提示偏对话性质，它不会调用任何工具、子 agent 之类；如果更复杂，它会动用一切手段给你答案。
——我还注意到模型保持了我们熟悉的个性，不应该突然变得“冷冰冰、机械、无聊”。

但真正糟糕的部分是：每一条消息都会计入你套餐的使用额度，除非你用的是 Instant 模型，那些不计入。

原文：Before OpenAI cut off accidental access to the merged Chat + Work experience, I managed to test it myself.

I've been getting a lot of questions about "what things will look like in December," and if it's anything like what I tested, here's how it'll work:

- We'll have a single Chat interface where we can talk to the model and choose from all the variants we already have (GPT-6 Sol, Luna, 6.1 Sol, Terra, etc.).
- Once you select a model, you can chat with it normally, and it'll be smart enough to figure out whether your message requires any tools.
- If your prompt is more conversational, it won't call any tools, subagents, or anything like that. But if it's something more complex, it'll use everything at its disposal to give you an answer.
- I also noticed that the model keeps the same personality we're used to, so it shouldn't suddenly become "cold, robotic, and boring."

But here's the really awful part: every single message will count against your plan's usage limits, unless you're using an Instant model. Those won't count.

2026-11-10 03:48 北京时间 ｜ Source: @kimmonismus ｜ 原帖链接

把现有的开源权重前沿模型在几小时内转换成恒定内存推理——这是 @i14labs 声称能做到的。

这家墨尔本实验室今天推出 Sugar Glider，一个每 token O(1) 推理、KV cache 大小固定的 LLM 架构家族。随着上下文增长，每 token 的内存和计算保持不变。i14 报告称已在最多约 1 万亿参数的模型上测试了该方法。

行业正在向数据中心、GPU 和电力投入巨额资金以满足需求。这种建设无法永远保持同样速度。电力和电网限制、施工延误以及对新数据中心日益增长的反对都在拖慢它。

因此，从现有硬件中榨取更多工作正在成为必需。i14 称其架构做到了这一点并保持前沿性能，我认为接下来的很多收益将恰恰来自这类工作。

原文：Take an existing open-weight frontier model and convert it to constant-memory inference in a matter of hours. That's what @i14labs says it can do.

The Melbourne lab launched Sugar Glider today, a family of LLM architectures with O(1) per-token inference and a fixed-size KV cache. Memory and compute per token stay flat as the context grows. i14 reports testing the approach on models up to roughly one trillion parameters.

The industry is pouring enormous sums into data centers, GPUs and power to keep up with demand. That buildout can't keep the same pace forever. Power and grid limits, construction delays and growing pushback against new data centers all slow it down.

So getting more work out of the hardware we already have is turning into a necessity. i14 says its architectures do that while keeping frontier performance, and I think a lot of the next gains will come from exactly this kind of work.

Waitlist is open at https://t.co/JUYidkbNxd

183 毫秒 vs Claude Opus 5.5 的 3.5 秒。

Drex Max 完全跳过文本生成。你给它一组选项加上文本、截图或图表，它一次前向就返回校准后的概率。@ArtificialAnlys 刚把 1.1 版排在其独立 Decision Index 决策模型第一。

原文：183 milliseconds vs. 3.5 seconds for Claude Opus 5.5.

That's the latency gap @NaceAI reports in its new paper on Drex Max, a 256k context decision model. GPT-6 Sol comes in around 2 seconds.

Drex Max skips text generation entirely. You hand it a set of options, plus text, screenshots or charts, and it returns calibrated probabilities in one pass. @ArtificialAnlys just ranked version 1.1 #1 among decision models on its independent Decision Index.

If you build agents that make hundreds of small choices per task, those seconds add up fast.

Anthropic 发布 Claude Fable 5.2，以 65 分登上 Artificial Analysis 智能指数 v5 第一，主要得益于科学、知识和编程方面的提升。

在 max effort 下得 65 分，领先 Gemini 4 Carbon（high，60）5 分、Claude Opus 5.5（max，59）6 分。提升来自科学推理、知识和 FrontierCode 2.0，在 AA-Briefcase 和 Terminal-Bench 4.0 上与 Opus 5.5 大致持平。

定价为每百万输入/输出 token 8/40 美元，比 Fable 5.1 降低 20%；但 max effort 下平均每任务成本 26.5 美元，约为 Opus 5.5（max，10.0 美元）的 2.6 倍、Fable 5.1（max，12.99 美元）的约 2 倍。

要点：
➤ 在智能指数 9 项评测中领先 5 项：max 下 65 分，领先 Gemini 4 Carbon（60）、Opus 5.5（59）和 GPT-6 Astra（max，57），比 Fable 5.1（max，49）高约 15 分。在 HLE-Diamond（80%，此前最佳为 Gemini 4 Carbon 的 69%）、AA-Omniscience v1.1（67，此前 54）、Terminal-Bench-Science（73%，此前 65%）、CritPt v2 困难子集（77%，此前 GPT-6 Astra 72%）和 FrontierCode 2.0（49%，此前 Opus 5.5 46%）上创最高分
➤ Agentic 知识工作和终端编程进展较小：有时仅达到 Opus 5.5 水平。AA-Briefcase 1785 Elo，在 Opus 5.5（1800）置信区间内，略低于 Sonnet 5.5（1813）；GDP.pdf 全通过率 24% vs Opus 5.5 的 26%；Terminal-Bench 4.0 60%，与 Opus 5.5 持平，落后 Kimi K3.1 Pro（67%）、Gemini 4 Carbon（65%）和 Sonnet 5.5（64%）。AutomationBench-AA 是例外，76% vs Opus 5.5 的 70%，略低于 Gemini 4 Carbon 的 78%
➤ 较低 effort 仍居领先：high 和 xhigh 下 62 分，超过任何其他模型的任何 effort，每任务 9.8 万输出 token，而 Opus 5.5（max）为 17.3 万。max 下 Fable 5.2 成本约为 Opus 5.5 的 2.6 倍，但在较低 effort 下可以以更低的每任务成本超过 Opus 5.5

其他：100 万 token 上下文，支持图像和文本输入；缓存写入 10 美元（5 分钟 TTL）/16 美元（1 小时 TTL），缓存读取 0.20 美元；五档 effort（low、medium、high、xhigh、max）。

原文：Anthropic has released Claude Fable 5.2, taking the #1 spot on the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index v5 at 65, led by gains in science, knowledge, and coding

At max effort it scores 65, 5 points ahead of Gemini 4 Carbon (high, 60) and 6 ahead of Claude Opus 5.5 (max, 59). Gains are driven by scientific reasoning, knowledge and FrontierCode 2.0, and roughly matching Opus 5.5 on AA-Briefcase and Terminal-Bench 4.0.

Priced at $8/$40 per 1M input/output tokens, a 20% reduction from Fable 5.1, Claude Fable 5.2 averages a Cost per Task of $26.5 with max effort, a ~2.6x increase compared to Opus 5.5 (max, $10.0) and ~2x compared to Fable 5.1 (max, $12.99)

Key takeaways:

➤ Claude Fable 5.2 leads 5 of the 9 evaluations in the Intelligence Index: At max effort, Fable 5.2 scores 65, ahead of Gemini 4 Carbon (high, 60), Claude Opus 5.5 (max, 59) and GPT-6 Astra (max, 57), and ~15 points above Claude Fable 5.1 (max, 49). It sets the highest scores on HLE-Diamond (80%, previous best 69% from Gemini 4 Carbon), AA-Omniscience v1.1 (67, previous best 54 from Gemini 4 Carbon), Terminal-Bench-Science (73%, previous best 65% from Gemini 4 Carbon), CritPt v2 hard subset (77%, previous best 72% from GPT-6 Astra) and FrontierCode 2.0 (49%, previous best 46% from Claude Opus 5.5)

➤ Less progress in agentic knowledge work and terminal coding: Claude Fable 5.2 improves on Fable 5.1 across capabilities, but sometimes only to Opus 5.5 levels. It reaches 1785 Elo on AA-Briefcase, within the confidence interval of Opus 5.5 (1800) and just behind Claude Sonnet 5.5 (1813). On GDP.pdf it reaches 24% all-pass against 26% for Opus 5.5. On Terminal-Bench 4.0 it scores 60%, level with Opus 5.5 and behind Kimi K3.1 Pro (67%), Gemini 4 Carbon (65%) and Sonnet 5.5 (64%). AutomationBench-AA is an exception, at 76% against 70% for Opus 5.5, narrowly behind Gemini 4 Carbon, 78%

➤ Lower efforts still take leading positions: With high and xhigh effort, Claude Fable 5.2 scores 62, ahead of every other model at any effort, using 98k output tokens per task against 173k for Opus 5.5 (max). At max effort Fable 5.2 costs ~2.6x more than Opus 5.5, but at lower effort settings it can outperform Opus 5.5 at lower cost per task

Other model details:

➤ Context window: 1 million tokens with image and text input

➤ Pricing: $8/$40 per 1M input/output tokens; cache writes $10 with 5-minute TTL, $16 with 1-hour TTL, and cache reads $0.20

➤ Effort settings: five (low, medium, high, xhigh, max). Intelligence Index evaluations were run at all five with Anthropic's default fallback enabled

Congratulations to @AnthropicAI on this impressive release!

Meta 最新模型 Muse Ember 在 Artificial Analysis 智能指数上得 36 分，与 Claude Haiku 5.5（max）持平，每任务成本仅三分之一。

Muse Ember 是 Meta 最新的高效档模型系列，输入输出 token 价格与 Claude Haiku 5.5 和 GPT-6 Luna 相同。

要点：
➤ 在 max 和 xhigh 两种 effort 下都位于智能指数-每任务成本帕累托前沿。Muse Ember（max）是得分 36 及以上最便宜的模型，每任务 0.14 美元；xhigh 下 34 分、0.12 美元。同分的 Claude Haiku 5.5（max）贵近 3 倍（0.41 美元）。Muse Ember（max）也比 DeepSeek V4.1 Flash 1017（max，33 分，0.60 美元）和 GLM-5.3-Flash（28 分，0.58 美元）便宜约 4 倍
➤ 比 Muse Wonder 低 8 分，但每任务成本约为其 1/50。Muse Wonder（max）44 分，每任务 6.95 美元
➤ 在 AutomationBench-AA 上突出：68.1%，与 Muse Wonder（max）持平；在 GDP.pdf 上胜过 Haiku 5.5（22.8% vs 20.8%），但在 AA-Briefcase 上落后（1521 vs 1574 Elo）
➤ Agentic 编程表现参差：Terminal-Bench 4.0 胜过 Haiku 5.5（40.9% vs 32.8%），FrontierCode 2.0 落后（27.2% vs 34.9%）
➤ 在自家编程 agent 中领先同价位模型：在 Muse Code 中 Coding Agent Index 46 分，领先 Claude Code 中的 Haiku 5.5（37）和 Codex 中的 GPT-6 Luna（31）
➤ 知识是主要弱点，但很少幻觉：AA-Omniscience 准确率 21.1%，在同等智能模型中最低（Haiku 5.5 为 34.7%，Gemini 3.8 Flash 为 50.5%），主要因为它拒答 64% 的问题，使幻觉率保持在 17.4%，为 Haiku 5.5 的一半

其他：定价每百万 token 输入 0.10 美元/缓存输入 0.004 美元/输出 0.50 美元；可通过 Meta 第一方 API 和 Muse Code 使用。

原文：Meta's newest model Muse Ember scores 36 on the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index, matching Claude Haiku 5.5 (max) at a third of the Cost per Task

Muse Ember is Meta's newest efficient-tier model line, with the same input and output token prices as Claude Haiku 5.5 and GPT-6 Luna

Key Takeaways:

➤ On the Intelligence Index vs. Cost per Task Pareto frontier at both max and xhigh effort. Muse Ember (max) is the cheapest model scoring 36 or higher, at $0.14 per task, and Muse Ember (xhigh) scores 34 for $0.12. Claude Haiku 5.5 (max) costs almost 3x more at $0.41 for the same score as Muse Ember (max). Muse Ember (max) is also about 4x cheaper than DeepSeek V4.1 Flash 1017 (max, 33, $0.60) and GLM-5.3-Flash (28, $0.58)

➤ 8 points behind Muse Wonder for about 1/50th of the Cost per Task. Muse Wonder (max) scores 44 at $6.95 per Intelligence Index task

➤ Stands out on AutomationBench-AA. Muse Ember (max) scores 68.1% on AutomationBench-AA, level with Muse Wonder (max), and beats Claude Haiku 5.5 (max) on GDP.pdf (22.8% vs 20.8%), but trails it on AA-Briefcase (1521 vs 1574 Elo)

➤ Mixed in agentic coding in our Intelligence Index evaluations. It beats Claude Haiku 5.5 (max) on Terminal-Bench 4.0 (40.9% vs 32.8%) but trails it on FrontierCode 2.0 (27.2% vs 34.9%)

➤ Ahead of similarly priced models when running in its own coding agent. In Muse Code, Muse Ember (max) scores 46 on the Artificial Analysis Coding Agent Index, ahead of Claude Haiku 5.5 in Claude Code (max, 37) and GPT-6 Luna in Codex (max, 31)

➤ Knowledge is the main weakness, but it rarely hallucinates. Its AA-Omniscience accuracy of 21.1% is the lowest of the similarly intelligent models we compared, against 34.7% for Claude Haiku 5.5 (max) and 50.5% for Gemini 3.8 Flash (high). This is mostly because it declines to answer 64% of questions, which keeps its hallucination rate at 17.4%, half of Claude Haiku 5.5’s (max)

Other details:
➤ Pricing: $0.10 input / $0.004 cached input / $0.50 output per 1M tokens
➤ Reasoning effort: results shown at max effort, with xhigh also on the Intelligence vs. Cost chart ➤ Availability: Meta's first-party API and Muse Code

2026-11-09 07:51 北京时间 ｜ Source: @ArtificialAnlys ｜ 原帖链接

推出 Artificial Analysis Decision Index v2：新的专有评测、更新的基准，以及一个奖励模型校准度的核心指标。

我们发布了第二版 Decision Index，以更好地反映真实世界效用并防范刷榜。指数中 45% 现在基于私有数据集（v1 为 25%）。@NaceAI 的 Drex Max v1.1 以 73 分在决策模型中小幅领先，与 GLiDE v1.2（@fastinoAI）和 Mammoth 27B（@baselabs）相差不到 1 分。

主要更新：新的 Decision Score（同时奖励正确性和校准后的置信度，每题 0–100，答对高于 50、答错低于 50）；新增私有基准 AA-Paperclip（30%，1500 份简历、收据、发票和 AI 论文等文档，15 个答案全对才算正确）；AA-Symmetry（15%，检验等价问题换种问法时答案是否一致）；AA-Omniscience-Decision v1.1 与 HLE-MultiChoice v1.1 权重各降至 15%；新增 EvalSafe-ONET-AA（15%），保留 NVIDIA 的 When2Call（降至 10%），移除已饱和的 WildGuardTest。

关键结果：三款决策模型在榜首相差 1 分以内（Drex Max v1.1 与 GLiDE v1.2 均 73，Mammoth 27B 72）；每百万任务成本差异超过 60 倍：Celeris-1.1 Decision（14 美元）、Decider v1.2（68 美元）和 Drex Max v1.1（102 美元）位于性价比帕累托前沿，Clef 为 859 美元；GLiDE v1.2 因每个问题都重复计费输入，成本 780 美元，约为 Drex Max 的 8 倍；语言模型得分更高但成本和耗时也更高：Claude Haiku 5.5（Max）77 分、GPT-6 Luna（Max）76 分，Luna 每百万任务 1530 美元（Drex Max 的 15 倍），每任务 11.34 秒 vs 275 毫秒（约慢 40 倍）。

原文：Introducing the Artificial Analysis Decision Index v2: a new proprietary evaluation, updated benchmarks, and a headline metric that rewards model calibration

We’ve released the second version of the Artificial Analysis Decision Index to better reflect real-world utility and guard against benchmark gaming. 45% of the Index is now built on private datasets, up from 25% in v1. Drex Max v1.1 from @NaceAI narrowly leads among decision models at 73, within 1 point of GLiDE v1.2 (@fastinoAI) and Mammoth 27B (@baselabs)

Updates in Decision Index v2:

➤ Decision Score: We adopt a new headline metric that rewards a model for both correctness and calibrated confidence. Each question scores from 0 to 100: right answers score above 50, wrong answers below 50, and confidence in its top answer moves the score within each half

➤ AA-Paperclip (30%): We added a new private benchmark of 1,500 documents including resumes, receipts, invoices, and AI and machine learning research papers. Models make decisions and extract facts from each document and are scored jointly as a single decision, correct only if all 15 answers are right

➤ AA-Symmetry (15%): This evaluates whether models change their answers for equivalent questions worded differently. We test 120 questions with variations such as reordered options, swapped labels, and negated yes/no questions, then measure how consistent the model’s probabilities are across those variations

➤ AA-Omniscience-Decision v1.1 and HLE-MultiChoice v1.1 (15% each, down from 25%): AA-Omniscience-Decision now varies the wording of its “I don’t know” option across ten alternatives. HLE-MultiChoice adds an option to decline using the same ten phrasings

We’ve also added EvalSafe-ONET-AA (15%), our implementation of @typesafeai’s ONET benchmark. We have retained When2Call from @NVIDIAAI in the Index at a decreased weighting of 10% (down from 25%), and removed WildGuardTest given its high saturation

Key results:

➤ Three decision models sit within 1 point at the top of the Index: Drex Max v1.1 from Nace AI and GLiDE v1.2 from Fastino Labs both score 73, with Mammoth 27B from Base Labs scoring 72

➤ Cost per 1M Tasks varies by more than 60x across decision models: Celeris-1.1 Decision ($14), Decider v1.2 ($68) and Drex Max v1.1 ($102) sit on the Decision Index vs. Cost Pareto frontier, while Clef costs $859. Token price is not the only cost driver: most decision models, including Drex Max v1.1 and Decider v1.2, bill a document’s input once for all its questions, while GLiDE v1.2 and Decider Flash v1.2 bill it for every question. This means GLiDE v1.2 costs $780, nearly 8x higher than Drex Max v1.1 despite only 33% higher list prices

➤ Language models can score higher, alongside higher Cost and Time per Task: Claude Haiku 5.5 (Max) scores 77 and GPT-6 Luna (Max) scores 76 on Decision Index v2, putting them ~3 points ahead of Drex Max v1.1. GPT-6 Luna (Max) costs $1,530 per 1M Tasks vs. $102 for Drex Max v1.1 (15x the cost) and takes 11.34 seconds per task vs. 275 milliseconds (~40x slower)

2026-11-10 03:45 北京时间 ｜ Source: @ArtificialAnlys ｜ 原帖链接

Fable 5.2 在 HLE-Diamond 上得 80%，领先 Gemini 4 Carbon 11 分，提升在知识题上最大（75% vs 59%），推理题为 86% vs 80%；AA-Omniscience v1.1 得 67，领先 Carbon 13 分、Opus 5.5 19 分；Terminal-Bench-Science 73%，领先 Carbon 8 分；CritPt v2 困难子集 77%，领先 GPT-6 Astra 5 分；在 @cognition 的私有评测 FrontierCode 2.0（维护者真正会合并的代码）上 49%，领先 Opus 5.5（max）3 分，high effort 与 max 相同。

原文：Claude Fable 5.2 leads 5 of the 9 evaluations in Intelligence Index v5, spanning science, knowledge and coding

On HLE-Diamond it scores 80%, 11 points ahead of Gemini 4 Carbon. The gain is largest on knowledge questions (75% vs 59% for Carbon), against reasoning (86% vs 80%).

On AA-Omniscience v1.1 it scores 67, 13 points ahead of Gemini 4 Carbon and 19 ahead of Opus 5.5.

On Terminal-Bench-Science it scores 73%, 8 points ahead of Gemini 4 Carbon, and on the CritPt v2 hard subset 77%, 5 points ahead of GPT-6 Astra.
