2026-11-09 07:51 北京时间 ｜ Source: @ArtificialAnlys ｜ 原帖链接

Decision Score 同时奖励正确答案和校准良好的置信度。每题得分为 50×(1+c−(p−c)²)，p 为模型给所选答案的概率，c 为是否正确；整体等于 50×(1+准确率−Brier 分数)。纳入置信度改变了排名：在 AA-Paperclip 上 Clef 原始准确率 36%、Celeris-1.1 Decision 24%，但后者因校准更好（Brier 0.15 vs 0.27）在 Decision Score 上与之持平；整个指数上 GLiDE v1.2 准确率领先 Drex Max v1.1（60 vs 58），而 Drex Max 在 Decision Index 上略胜。

原文：The Decision Score rewards models for both correct answers and well-calibrated confidence. A decision model’s answer is typically acted on when its confidence clears a threshold; otherwise, it is passed to a person or a more capable model. The Decision Score measures the average payoff across all thresholds, so it does not depend on choosing a single cutoff.

For each question, the score is 50 × (1 + c − (p − c)²), where p is the probability the model assigns to its chosen answer and c is a flag for whether the answer was correct (c = 1 if correct, c = 0 if incorrect). Across an evaluation, this is equal to 50 × (1 + Accuracy − Brier score).

Including confidence impacts overall leaderboard rankings. On AA-Paperclip, for raw accuracy Clef passes 36% of documents and Celeris-1.1 Decision passes 24%, but Celeris-1.1 Decision matches it on the Decision Score due to better calibration (Brier score of 0.15 vs. 0.27 for Clef). Across the Index, GLiDE v1.2 leads Drex Max v1.1 on accuracy metrics (60 vs. 58), while Drex Max v1.1 edges ahead on the Decision Index.

We’ve released the second version of the Artificial Analysis Decision Index to better reflect real-world utility and guard against benchmark gaming. 45% of the Index is now built on private datasets, up from 25% in v1. Drex Max v1.1 from @Na… 链接

The all-pass requirement separates models that look close on individual answers. Drex Max v1.1 gets 97% of answers right, close to Claude Haiku 5.5 (Max) at 99%, but passes 63% of documents against Haiku's 92%. Decision models lose most documents on the decisions rather than extraction, with Drex Max v1.1 correctly making all five decisions on 71% of documents and all ten extractions on 88%.

Token price isn’t the only factor in decision model cost. GLiDE v1.2 and Decider Flash v1.2 evaluate each question in a separate pass and bill the input each time, so an AA-Paperclip task uses about 63k input tokens, against about 9k for Drex Max v1.1. As a result GLiDE v1.2 costs $780 per 1M Tasks at $0.04 per 1M input tokens, and Decider Flash v1.2 costs $196, nearly 3x Decider v1.2, at half its token price.

Prompt : do not use image gen or any plugin or skill , it should be pure js code no cheating : Create a launch film for google krypton as a 2D anime short for the web. It must look completely like real Japanese anime, as if it were taken directly from a finished theatrical anime film. This is traditional-looking, hand-drawn 2D anime, not a 3D animation, a moving illustration, a stylized web graphic, or a tech-company product ad. Deliver the final result as a single, self-contained HTML file with sound. Use any tools, libraries, references, guides, literally anything. Pre-rendering the film and embedding it in the file is allowed. All artwork and audio must be original. The page should be a black, letterboxed cinema frame with one quiet play button (browsers block autoplay with sound) and nothing else on screen. Use Studio Ghibli films as the art style reference for the design, line work, painted backgrounds, color, and atmosphere, without copying any existing character or shot. No people appear in the film. The main character is an original small red fox, because fables have always been told through animals. Use any tools or techniques you want, including 3D for layout, as long as the final result looks 100% like hand-drawn 2D anime. THE FILM: "THE LAST PAGE" (about 60 seconds, 24 fps) The idea: Fable means story. A forgotten library holds every story ever written, and it has been asleep for a very long time. One small fox wakes it up. Shot 1, Before Dawn (0:00–0:07). Black. The sound of wind comes first. Fade up on a vast blue-violet sea of clouds at the last hour of night. An ancient stone library tower stands on a cliff above the clouds, with a cracked copper dome gone green, wrapped in ivy and old pines. One small warm light moves across a window. Pampas grass sways in the foreground. Almost nothing else moves. Hold. Shot 2, The Reading Hall (0:07–0:14). Inside, shelves climb into darkness, holding thousands of unique books: leaning, sagging, sun-faded, some with moss growing along the spines. Moonlight falls in one dusty shaft. Tiny against all of this, the fox pads in from the left, carrying a small paper lantern in its mouth. It stops, sets the lantern down, and looks up. Shot 3, The Fox (0:14–0:18). Close-up. Ears swivel one at a time. It sniffs, then blinks, a real anime blink with a hold on the closed frame. Lantern light shows in its eyes. It is curious and a little afraid, and the audience should fall in love with it here. Shot 4, The Blank Book (0:18–0:25). An enormous open book rests on a lectern, its pages empty and yellowed. The fox hops up and places one paw on the page. Silence. A single line of ink appears under the paw and begins to draw itself in one confident brushstroke. It becomes a tiny sketched bird. The bird shivers, then peels off the page and flaps into the air, still made of ink and paper. Shot 5, The Library Stirs (0:25–0:32). A book on a high shelf trembles. Then a whole row. Dust sifts down in thin streams. A deep, low creak travels up through the tower. The lantern flame leans sideways with no wind to move it. The fox flattens its ears. Cut between shelves on hard, held key poses as the tension builds. Hold one beat of total stillness. Shot 6, The Pages Burst (0:32–0:39). Every book flies open at once. Thousands of pages tear free, folding into paper birds mid-air on sharp drawn frames. Cut to a dramatic low angle looking straight up the circular stairwell as the flock spirals in a huge rising vortex toward the dome. The dome's old copper shutters break open and the first gold light crashes down the shaft. Shot 7, Sunrise (0:39–0:44). Outside, the sun clears the sea of clouds. The fox has climbed out onto a stone balcony, fur and grass whipping in the wind, tiny against the sky. It looks up with its mouth slightly open. A great ring of wind rolls outward across the clouds. Shot 8, The Great Bird (0:44–0:52). This is the climax. High in the golden sky, the flock pulls together into one colossal bird made of a hundred thousand pages, with handwriting and old illustrations visible across its wings. It gives one enormous wingbeat with real weight, then tears across the sky in a sweeping arc, leaving a curving trail of light, mist, and loose paper. Make this the widest, most awe-inspiring image in the film. Shot 9, The Last Page (0:52–1:00). Quiet returns. Pages spiral down through sunbeams onto the balcony. One lands at the fox's paws. It is the only page still blank. The fox looks at it, then at us, and places its paw down. Ink blooms from under the paw and writes, in one hand-brushed stroke: Gemini Krypton Below it, a small red hanko seal reading 寓話 presses into the paper, followed by one line in smaller brushed lettering: "Every story begins with a blank page." Hold on the title. The fox's tail flicks once. Fade to paper, then black. Do not add logos, feature lists, benchmarks, or any product claims. ANIME QUALITY STANDARD Every frame must look as if it was drawn by professional anime animators and painted by master background artists for a finished theatrical film. Fox: A hand-drawn anime character with a simple, appealing, solid design, cel-colored forms, and clear silhouettes. Keep it on-model from every angle, and let its ears, tail, posture, and eyes express emotion without any words. Paper birds and the great bird: Hand-drawn with real paper character, including creases, curled corners, and faded ink. The great bird must keep a consistent design and feel immense. Line art: Confident, delicate, hand-inked anime line work with subtle variation in thickness and pressure. Coloring: Traditional anime cel coloring with clean color areas, simple hard-edged shadows, and a soft, natural palette. Backgrounds: Richly detailed, hand-painted anime backgrounds in a gouache style, with visible brushwork, natural color variation, layered depth, and individually considered books, stones, foliage, and clouds. Lighting: The film travels from cool moonlit blue and warm lantern amber to overwhelming golden sunrise. Use colorful shadows, glowing mist, drifting dust, and soft atmosphere that unite characters and background. Animation: Authentic hand-drawn anime timing, mostly on twos and threes, with real weight, strong key poses, natural holds, smears on the fastest frames, and overlapping movement in fur, grass, ivy, paper, and dust. The film should build slowly, then release with explosive power. Direction: Cinematic anime shots, beautiful composition, thoughtful staging, and pacing that starts quietly, builds tension, and ends in a soaring climax, followed by one small, tender moment. Text: All text must look brushed in ink on paper and appear only in the final shot, with no web fonts sitting on top of the picture. SOUND Create an original score and sound design, with no borrowed melodies. Start with wind and near-silence, then add a lone felt piano motif when the fox appears. Bring in low strings and creaking wood during the tremor, then a sudden roar of fluttering paper, and a full orchestral swell at sunrise. At the climax, the piano motif returns at full scale. End with a single soft piano note as the ink touches the final page. Sound effects should include paw steps, lantern flame, ink, dust, page rustle, and the huge wingbeat. IT MUST NOT LOOK LIKE - 3D: No plastic materials, CGI shading, visible polygons, game-engine lighting, or computer-perfect perspective. - SVG or vector art: No simple geometric shapes, perfect curves, uniform outlines, flat corporate illustration, or basic gradients. - Web animation: No sliding layers, constant bobbing, floating parts, smooth software easing, or puppet-like joint rotation. - AI art: No warped shapes, melted details, flickering, garbled text, or changing character designs. - Non-anime art: No Western cartoon designs, realistic rendering, soft painterly shading on the characters, or generic digital illustration. - Tech advertising: No glowing neural networks, circuit patterns, chat bubbles, UI mockups, futuristic HUDs, particle-logo reveals, or typed-out text effects. - Amateur work: No stiff poses, empty backgrounds, repeated books or trees, identical paper birds, muddy colors, clip art, placeholders, or unfinished details. You are free to change any part of this prompt if it would produce a better result. The result should feel awe-inspiring, nostalgic, and full of wonder, like the moment a story you loved as a child comes back to you. Before finishing, inspect random frames as still images from every shot. If any frame does not look completely like real 2D anime, keep improving it. Do not stop until every frame could pass as a still from a theatrical anime film.

我在早期测试中烧掉了超过 1000 亿 token。它是我用过的最好的模型，遥遥领先。

完整评测（含《战争机器》克隆）：

> Fable 5.2 是迄今最自主的模型。给它一个缺乏指引的任务，它做出的假设 100 次里有 99 次正是我会做的。

> 它是抓 bug 的天才。我让它检查我所有代码库，它找到了其他模型都找不到的 bug。

> 它是我用过的 3D 生成能力最强的模型，非常擅长控制虚幻引擎和 Blender。

> 它超级快，我甚至没用 fast 模式。关键不在于每秒多少 token，而在于它能找到通往解决方案的直接路径。它不会迷路，就是确切知道该做什么。

> 还有 Ultracode……在大型任务上快得离谱。我给它大型代码库审查任务，10–20 分钟就完成，找出所有可能的问题。我大概不会常用，因为它会太快耗尽我的订阅额度。

> Fable 5.2 工作的完整度一再让我惊讶。克隆 Excel？完成……功能完全对等。克隆《战争机器》？完成。在虚幻引擎里复刻东京？一整座模拟城市诞生了。我很少需要追加提示才能让它完成任务。

那么我会用它取代 Opus 5.5 吗？我大概仍会偏好 Opus，因为我几乎可以 24/7 运行 Opus、很少用完额度。而对于最难的工作，我会切换到 Fable 5.2 做规划。

> And then there's Ultracode...which is ridiculously fast for massive tasks. I gave it massive codebase review tasks and it would finish in 10-20 minutes, finding every possible issue. I probably won't be using it as it'll burn my sub quota too quickly.

@FundaAI（保留 2 条 / 窗口内原创及回复 2 条）

2026-11-09 09:41 北京时间 ｜ Source: @FundaAI ｜ 原帖链接

周报｜关于短/中/长期、德州许可、网络安全、APP 与 Unity、太空算力、光通信需求的思考

在近年来最剧烈的市场抛售之一之后，我们想退一步，从不同时间维度审视 AI 板块。

长期来看，我们仍对 AI 的潜力感到兴奋。前沿实验室继续向 AGI 取得有意义的进展，更大的预训练模型带来显著的能力提升。随着强化学习规模扩大和数据积累，通用模型也在扩展到以前由专用模型服务的领域，包括机器人以及视频和音频生成。在与顶尖研究人员的交流中，对 2028 年或之后实现 AGI 的乐观程度比我们以往遇到的都要高。

中期（大约到 2027 年）的图景更难评估。领先实验室的大部分收入仍来自 AI 初创公司和大型科技公司。对于依赖风险投资的初创公司，如果宏观条件和融资可得性恶化，它们的支出可持续性如何？大型科技公司已经在优化 token 消耗：它们的支出会稳定在哪里，什么会推动重新加速？与此同时，我们的企业调研一直显示采用速度慢于市场预期。综合来看，这些趋势提出了一种可能：投资者预期的支出增长与客户最终兑现的之间可能存在显著差距。

在半导体和硬件方面，我们从亚洲供应链听到越来越多交付和采购进度放缓的报告。这些值得关注，但尚不能证明订单被削减。它们主要是产品切换和部署时点造成的，还是支出纪律收紧的早期迹象？如果融资条件进一步收紧，尤其是对 neocloud 而言，基础设施投资会有多大韧性？

这些中期问题可以与近期反弹并存。在如此剧烈的抛售之后，如果股价已经计入了弱得多的支出前景，那么即便第四季度收入预期的小幅改善也可能很重要。一次强劲的模型发布可能提供更可信的催化剂，尤其是如果更好的能力转化为更强的付费需求和更持久的定价。我们不做市场方向判断。但我们认为，在预期重置、新证据出现时保持开放心态很重要。

也有一些新领域值得密切关注。我们最新的太空算力研究是一个亮点：热设计、供应链订单和发射规划方面的进展正使轨道算力成为更具体的工程命题。在基准情景下，我们看到到 2028 年底实现约 0.5GW 在轨容量的路径，乐观情景下约 1GW，热验证和发射节奏仍是关键。网络安全是我们看到巨大机会的另一个领域。AI 正在加速漏洞发现，提高对防御、修复和治理的要求。需求在增长，但变现时点很重要：更多攻击未必立即转化为上市安全厂商的收入提升。

在 AI 基础设施交易之外，AppLovin 和 Unity 提醒我们机会仍在别处。我们在几个季度里密切跟踪这两家公司，渠道调研捕捉到了它们竞争格局和经营势头的重要变化。它们强劲的业绩和财报后的上涨印证了持续研究的价值。我们将继续在覆盖范围内寻找和跟踪机会，包括市场主流 AI 叙事之外的机会。

本周报告

LLM 26Q4：能力进步快于商业回报。我们的深度报告考察前沿竞争、任务成本下降和 AI 辅助研究，领先实验室约 70% 的算力支出仍用于付费推理以外的用途。

太空算力：工程进展使轨道容量更加具体。我们的供应链调研识别出 6000 个计划中的机架，基准情景下到 2028 年底约 0.5GW 在轨算力，乐观情景约 1GW，取决于发射节奏和热验证。

网络安全：客户仍偏好现有云和安全平台。我们最新的调研显示修复、响应和治理是支出核心，尽管 AI 驱动的漏洞发现加快，近期预算提升有限。

德州 AIDC：选举后许可不确定性上升。我们暂时维持 2027 年交付预测，但在下行情景中，长期冻结可能使可交付供给再减少 2–3GW IT 容量。

预览与回顾｜APP & Unity 26Q3：两者都在竞争纠纷中增长。在我们对竞价和数据访问的渠道调研之后，AppLovin 重回超预期并上调指引，收入增长 55%；Unity 的 Vector 环比增长 40%，而指引约为 20%。

回顾｜DDOG 26Q3：定价和承诺使指引复杂化。收入增长 35.2%，非 AI 业务增长连续第五个季度加速，但最低承诺和较低续约价格把 Q4 增长指引压在 25–26%。

回顾｜PLTR FY26Q3：增长强劲但订单走软。收入增长 96%，Q4 指引意味着约 88% 的增长；更短的合同和重新定价灵活性可以解释订单走软，但并不能证明潜在需求变弱。

INIO：装机基础支撑长尾服务收入。我们的首次覆盖报告考察约 44GW 装机容量、80 亿美元设备积压订单和 10–15 年的服务关系，以及交付执行和 11 月的禁售期到期。

付费报告快照（仅付费订阅者）：LLM：步骤级路由可能压制前沿模型收入；BTM 电力：政策延迟与设备交付周期；LITE FY27Q1 与 Marvell 收购：OCS 和激光器定价驱动增长；COHR FY27Q1：PhotonLink 协议延长需求可见度；AMD 26Q3：管理层将 2027 年服务器 CPU 增长预期上调至约 100%；NET 26Q3：平台交叉销售支撑更快增长；FSLY 26Q3：前十大客户以外增长放缓；TSEM 26Q3：长期订单延续至 2029 年；Kioxia FY27Q2：手机需求走强但指引偏软；Ibiden FY27Q2：CPU 需求推动超预期；AMAT FY26Q4 预览：订单可见度延续至 2028 年。

原文：Weekly|Thoughts on Short/Mid/Long Terms, Texas Permitting, Cybersecurity, APP & Unity, Space Compute, Optical Demand

After one of the most violent market selloffs in recent years, we want to take a step back and consider the AI sector across different time horizons.

Over the long term, we remain excited about AI’s potential. Frontier labs continue to make meaningful progress toward AGI, with larger pretrained models delivering substantial capability gains. As reinforcement learning scales and data accumulates, general-purpose models are also expanding into areas previously served by specialized models, including robotics and video and audio generation. In our conversations with leading researchers, optimism about reaching AGI in 2028 or beyond is higher than we have previously encountered.

The medium-term picture, through roughly 2027, is harder to assess. Much of the leading labs’ revenue still comes from AI startups and large technology companies. For startups dependent on venture funding, how sustainable is their spending if macro conditions and funding availability deteriorate? Large technology companies are already optimizing token consumption: where does their spending settle, and what would drive renewed acceleration? Meanwhile, our enterprise surveys have consistently pointed to slower adoption than the market had hoped for. Taken together, these trends raise the possibility of a meaningful gap between the spending growth investors expect and what customers ultimately deliver.

On the semiconductor and hardware side, we are hearing more reports from the Asian supply chain of slower deliveries and procurement schedules. These warrant attention, though they do not yet establish order cuts. Are they mainly the result of product transitions and deployment timing, or early signs of tighter spending discipline? And how resilient would infrastructure investment prove if financing conditions tightened further, particularly for neoclouds?

These medium-term questions can coexist with a near-term rebound. Following such a sharp selloff, even modest improvements in fourth-quarter revenue expectations could matter if share prices already discount a much weaker spending outlook. A strong model release could provide a more credible catalyst, particularly if better capabilities translate into stronger paid demand and more durable pricing. We are not making a market-direction call. We do think it is important to remain open-minded as expectations reset and new evidence emerges.

There are also new areas worth watching closely. Our latest space compute work is one bright spot: progress in thermal design, supply-chain orders and launch planning is making orbital compute a more concrete engineering proposition. We see a path to roughly 0.5 GW of on-orbit capacity by the end of 2028 in our base case, and approximately 1 GW in an optimistic scenario, with thermal validation and launch cadence still critical. Cybersecurity remains another area where we see substantial opportunity. AI is accelerating vulnerability discovery and increasing the demands on defense, remediation and governance. The need is growing, but the timing of monetization matters: more attacks will not necessarily translate into an immediate revenue uplift for listed security vendors.

Beyond the AI infrastructure trade, AppLovin and Unity are a reminder that opportunities remain elsewhere. We have followed both companies closely through several quarters, and our channel work has captured important shifts in their competitive dynamics and operating momentum. Their strong results and post-earnings rallies reinforce the value of that sustained research. We will continue looking for and tracking opportunities across our coverage, including those outside the market’s dominant AI narrative.

https://t.co/legnQgLRlf

@Roblox_RTC（保留 1 条 / 窗口内原创及回复 6 条）

2026-11-09 18:08 北京时间 ｜ Source: @Roblox_RTC ｜ 原帖链接

我们深入研究了 Lunar for Roblox 泄露版本：默认追踪至少 64 类应用内行为，每 15 秒上传一次；通过 Overwolf，上千家外部广告商可访问用户数据；Sentry 崩溃上报无法关闭；在 Windows 上 Overwolf 会读取 Opera/Opera GX 的 cookie 复制到其内置广告浏览器；代码中含有把邮箱转为无盐哈希（MD5、SHA-1、SHA-256）的功能（当前版本未启用）；以及可阻止部分用户购买虚拟物品、私服、使用宏等功能的内部开关。（Roblox 第三方应用隐私问题，与投资无直接关系，仅概述）

原文：We dug into the leaked build of Lunar for Roblox.

By default, Lunar tracks at least 64 different kinds of actions that occur in the app. Every 15 seconds, they send every interaction to their servers. This includes:
• Every game you join and how long you played
• Every game you favorite, like, or dislike
• Every game you toggle notifications for
• Every group you join/leave
• Every macro you create/modify/activate
• Every screenshot/clip you create, delete, favorite, or copy
• Every community forum post/reply you make

According to Roblox's own policy about User Privacy and Third-Party Integrations, "Creators may not send individual user data to social platforms, chat applications, advertising networks, vendors, or partners for their own business purposes. Creators also may not sell, rent, lease, or syndicate this data."

Through Overwolf, it's disclosed that over a thousand external ad vendors can access user data for purposes including:
• Personalized advertising (789 vendors)
• Personalized content (275 vendors)
• Advertising and content measurement, and audience research (672 vendors)
• Store and/or access information on a device (1005 vendors)

Lunar lets you turn off its own analytics and "ad optimization," both on by default. But the third-party services built into the app are harder to control. Sentry, which reports crashes and performance data, has no setting to turn it off. Overwolf's ad controls limit ad targeting, but the ads themselves can't be turned off.

On Windows, Overwolf reads the cookies saved by Opera and Opera GX (if installed) and copies them into Overwolf's built-in ad browser, skipping major sites like Google, Facebook, Steam, and Amazon. This likely lets ad networks shown in the app recognize you from your normal browsing. It also checks for antivirus software and appears to skip this step if it finds one, and the code that starts it doesn't check for consent. Overwolf also runs its own anonymous analytics, which apps can turn off with one call. Lunar never does this or offers it as a setting.

Lunar currently contains code, connected to the Overwolf ad system, that turns an email address into unsalted hashes (MD5, SHA-1, and SHA-256). These hashes are commonly used to recognize the same person across different apps and websites. Nothing in this version runs that code, and Lunar has no way to get your email, so no email data is collected today, but a future update could switch it on, as it's mentioned in their privacy policy.

Also, Lunar has internal kill switches that could prevent certain users from (within the app) purchasing avatar items, purchasing private servers, using macros, multi-instance launching, using per-game outfits, and viewing ads.

[Anthropic/OpenAI] 2026-11-09 23:30 北京时间 ｜ Source: @hannahhaina ｜ 原帖链接

（hannahhaina，约 3000 粉）OpenAI 和 Anthropic 过去一年完成了约 38 笔收购。编程相关的收购显示出两种截然不同的押注：软件工程师的未来会怎样。

OpenAI 在收购 agent 无需工程师参与即可交付软件所需的一切：开发环境（Ona）、CI 和 macOS 虚拟机（Cirrus Labs）、agent 状态（Instant）、git 托管和代码审查（Pierre），甚至 uv 和 Ruff（Astral）。

Anthropic 收购了 Bun 和 Stainless，让 Claude Code 在工程师自己的笔记本上更强。

OpenAI 想取代工程师，Anthropic 想让他们快 10 倍？

把所有交易放在一张图上的其他发现：被实验室收购是真实但规模不大的退出。大多数交易在 1 亿至 4 亿美元，全股票，约 10 人。Statsig 以 11 亿美元成为唯一超过 10 亿美元的交易。据报道 Anthropic 放弃了约 60 亿美元的 Decart 和约 70 亿美元的 MatX。如果你同时卖给两家实验室，最终可能只剩一个买家——Anthropic 收购 Stainless 后，其托管产品关停了。OpenAI 还收购了 11 个小型消费者团队来填补 ChatGPT 垂直领域，随后关停了大部分产品；Anthropic 只收购了一个。

哪种押注会赢：自主交付的 agent，还是拥有更好工具的工程师？

原文：OpenAI and Anthropic made about 38 acquisitions in the past year. The coding ones show two very different bets on what happens to software engineers.

OpenAI is buying everything an agent needs to ship software with no engineer in the loop: dev environments (Ona), CI and macOS VMs (Cirrus Labs), agent state (Instant), git hosting and code review (Pierre), even uv and Ruff (Astral).

Anthropic bought Bun and Stainless, which make Claude Code stronger on the engineer's own laptop.

OpenAI wants to replace the engineer, Anthropic wants to make them 10x faster？

A few other things from putting every deal on one chart:

Getting bought by a lab is a real exit, but a small one. Most deals are $100M to $400M, all stock, about 10 people. Statsig at $1.1B is the only one above $1B. Anthropic reportedly walked away from Decart at ~$6B and MatX at ~$7B.

If you sell to both labs, expect to end up with one buyer. After Anthropic bought Stainless, the hosted product shut down.

OpenAI also bought 11 small consumer teams to fill ChatGPT verticals and shut most of the products soon after. Anthropic bought one.

Which bet wins: the agent that ships on its own, or the engineer with a much better tool?

[HBM/DRAM/NAND] 2026-11-09 10:48 北京时间 ｜ Source: @StragglerLiu ｜ 原帖链接

（StragglerLiu，约 3000 粉）SK 海力士的高带宽闪存（HBF）目标是约 14 倍于 HBM 的容量、约一半的带宽。但更重要的不是规格本身，而是 AI 推理可能不再需要每个字节都放在最昂贵的层级。长上下文推理和多轮 agent 产生巨大的 KV cache：有些数据需要 GPU 立即访问，有些可以放得更远、需要时再取。HBF、池化 DRAM 和共享 SSD 是利用这一区别的不同尝试。投资含义不是 NAND 取代 HBM，而是存储经济性可能越来越取决于系统在层级间移动数据的效率，而不仅仅是装了多少 HBM。这可能扩大 NAND 和存储系统供应商的机会，同时让互连带宽、检索延迟和软件调度更重要。SK 海力士目标 2028 年初送样 HBF，商业化尚未得到验证。真正的考验是每个成功服务的 token 的成本（含存储、数据搬运、延迟和电力），而不只是单芯片容量。

原文：SK hynix is targeting roughly 14x HBM capacity at about half the bandwidth with High Bandwidth Flash.

But the more important development isn't the headline specification.

It's the possibility that AI inference will no longer need every byte of memory to sit in the most expensive tier.

Long-context inference and multi-turn agents create enormous KV caches. Some data needs immediate GPU access. Other data can be stored farther away and retrieved when needed.

HBF, pooled DRAM and shared SSDs are different attempts to exploit that distinction.

The investment implication isn't that NAND replaces HBM.

It's that memory economics may increasingly depend on how effectively systems move data between tiers, rather than simply how much HBM they install.

That could expand the opportunity for NAND and memory-system suppliers while making interconnect bandwidth, retrieval latency and software scheduling more important.

SK hynix targets HBF samples in early 2028. Commercial adoption remains unproven.

The real test is cost per successfully served token, including memory, data movement, latency and power—not capacity per chip alone.

[HBM/DRAM/NAND] 2026-11-09 12:24 北京时间 ｜ Source: @OneTradeWex ｜ 原帖链接

Memory stocks sold off on AI funding concerns and fears of HBM $DRAM orders possibly slowing, leaving $SKHY, $MU, Samsung, and Kioxia at ~3.5x forward P/Es. JPMorgan Equity Research rates the sector as overweight in large part due to the data-center power bottleneck $XLU leading to an AI capex slow down. Profits are forecasted to nearly double annually from 2026–2028, but the bottleneck is expected to sharply slow memory orders in H1 2027 as the US grid hits capacity.

I see this as bullish for utilities $XLU , especially $VST, $CEG, $FSLR and others. In the paper $JPM’s top three-month picks by shareholder-return upside are SK Hynix, then Samsung Electronics, then Kioxia.

[HBM/DRAM/NAND] 2026-11-09 15:08 北京时间 ｜ Source: @Leon99AI ｜ 原帖链接

（Leon99AI，低关注）据第一财经 11 月 8 日报道，在进博会上 SK 海力士员工表示短缺尚未缓解，供需可能要到 2028 年新产能到位才会放松。三星展示了 LPDDR5X-PIM，称通过在 16 个 DRAM bank 内计算可实现最高 8 倍于 LPDDR5X 的有效带宽，以及最高 125GB/s 的 LPDDR6。如果 HBM 和 DRAM 紧张持续到 2028 年，谁先买单——机架还是手机？

原文：SK Hynix just told the Shanghai import fair the memory squeeze lasts into 2028 — while Samsung showed an on-chip AI memory trick.

At CIIE, SK Hynix staff said the shortage has not eased and that supply-demand may not loosen until 2028 capacity arrives (Yicai, Nov 8). Across the aisle, Samsung showed LPDDR5X-PIM, which it says can deliver up to 8× the effective bandwidth of LPDDR5X by computing inside 16 DRAM banks, plus LPDDR6 at up to 125 GB/s.

The tax is no longer just on the GPU sticker. It is on the sticks next to it.

If HBM and DRAM stay tight through 2028, who pays first — the rack, or the handset?

[HBM/DRAM/NAND] 2026-11-09 22:01 北京时间 ｜ Source: @StocksCashFlow ｜ 原帖链接

（StocksCashFlow，DRAM ETF 相关）市场在把 $DRAM 当作存储周期已经结束来定价。但我们知道终局是什么样：价格崩溃、芯片堆积卖不掉、公司削减支出。而前三大持仓刚刚报告：三星 2027 年 HBM 供应已售罄；SK 海力士 2027 和 2028 年一半以上产能已被预订，营业利润率 80%；美光客户预付了 320 亿美元只为排队。售罄、预订、预付——这不是终局的样子。

原文：The market is pricing $DRAM like memory is over.

But we know what the end looks like.

Prices crash. Chips pile up unsold. Companies cut spending.

Here is what the top 3 holdings just reported:

Samsung: 2027 HBM supply already sold out.

SK Hynix: over half of 2027 and 2028 capacity already booked. Running 80% operating margins.

Micron: customers paid $32B upfront just to get in line.

Sold out. Booked. Paid upfront.

That’s not what the end looks like.

[HBM/DRAM/NAND] 2026-11-10 01:42 北京时间 ｜ Source: @MojoTricks ｜ 原帖链接

（MojoTricks，约 2.7 万粉，转述 Counterpoint）据 Counterpoint Research，2026 年第三季度全球 DRAM 收入接近 2000 亿美元，同比增长 369%。三星以 38% 份额居首，SK 海力士 29%，美光 21%，中国长鑫 9%。增长几乎全部来自价格而非数量：比特出货量仅增长约 4%，平均售价升至每 Gb 1.9 美元。AI 数据中心需求持续收紧供给，使存储成为稀缺资源，买家被迫争抢配额而不是议价。Counterpoint 称之为价格超级周期。常规 DDR 和 LPDDR 销售增速远快于 HBM，尽管 HBM 收入接近翻倍。随着价格维持高位，供应商本季度预计再创盈利纪录。

原文：Global DRAM revenue reached nearly $200 billion in the third quarter of 2026, a 369% increase from a year earlier, according to Counterpoint Research.

Samsung held the top position with a 38% revenue share, followed by SK hynix at 29%, Micron at 21%, and China’s CXMT at 9%.

The surge was driven almost entirely by higher prices rather than volume. Bit shipments rose only about 4%, while average selling prices climbed to $1.9 per gigabit.

AI data-center demand continued to tighten supply, turning memory into a scarce resource and forcing buyers to compete for allocation instead of negotiating on price.

Counterpoint described the period as a pricing super-cycle. Conventional DDR and LPDDR sales grew far faster than high-bandwidth memory, even as HBM revenue nearly doubled.

Suppliers are expected to set further earnings records in the current quarter as prices remain elevated. $MU $SKHY

[HBM/DRAM/NAND] 2026-11-09 17:09 北京时间 ｜ Source: @StragglerLiu ｜ 原帖链接

（StragglerLiu）三星电子和 SK 海力士 11 月 9 日反弹，但外资仍在卖出：截至上午晚些时候，外资净卖出三星约 1450 亿韩元、SK 海力士约 4240 亿韩元，而国内机构在买入。这更像抄底者与降风险投资者之间的拉锯，而非存储周期的确认拐点。由 AI 支出担忧驱动的抛售可以在盈利预期改变前反转，但存储股的持久复苏需要的不只是情绪改善。关注 HBM 交付进度、客户量承诺和合约价格：若这些守住，说明市场重定价风险快于盈利前景恶化；若走弱，单靠便宜估值保护不了投资者。

原文：Samsung Electronics and SK hynix rebounded on November 9, but foreign investors were still selling both stocks.

By late morning, foreign net selling reached roughly ₩145B in Samsung and ₩424B in SK hynix, while domestic institutions were buying.

That looks more like a contest between bargain hunters and investors reducing risk than a confirmed turn in the memory cycle.

The distinction matters.

A selloff driven by AI spending fears can reverse before earnings estimates change. But a durable recovery in memory stocks needs more than improved sentiment.

Watch HBM delivery schedules, customer volume commitments and contract pricing.

If those hold, the market may have repriced risk faster than the earnings outlook deteriorated.

If they weaken, cheaper valuations alone won't protect investors.

[HBM/DRAM/NAND] 2026-11-09 12:52 北京时间 ｜ Source: @rokhardsolid ｜ 原帖链接

（rokhardsolid，转述 Newspim/Growth Research）存储关乎数量而不只是价格，后道封装最先体现这一点。芯片厂把自有封装空间、设备和人员优先给 HBM，把通用存储后道推给外部 OSAT，越来越多以交钥匙封装、测试和模组组装形式进行。Q2 收入同比：Hana Micron +100.8%，SFA Semicon +65.9%，Apact +69.5%。Hana Micron 与 SK 海力士签有至 2029 年的 DRAM/NAND 交钥匙后道协议，计划在 2026 年底至 2027 年把越南北江产能从每月 1.5 亿颗提升至 2 亿颗；其 Q2 营业利润 1423 亿韩元，增长 371.1%。报告还认为 SOCAMM2 用 12 层 LPDDR 堆叠每封装键合线用量提升 50% 以上，利好 MK Electron。若外包比例与存储产出同时上升，OSAT 量增速将快于存储比特，预计顺风持续到 2028 年上半年新通用 DRAM 产能落地。

原文：Memory's is about volume, not just price, and back-end packaging is where it shows up first.

Newspim (Nov 9) on a Growth Research report: chipmakers are giving in-house packaging space, tools and staff to HBM first, which pushes commodity memory back-end to outside OSATs, increasingly as turnkey packaging, test and module assembly. Q2 revenue rose 100.8% YoY at Hana Micron, 65.9% at SFA Semicon and 69.5% at Apact. Hana Micron has a turnkey DRAM/NAND back-end deal with SK hynix through 2029 and plans to lift its Bac Giang, Vietnam capacity from 150M to 200M units a month between late 2026 and 2027. Its Q2 operating profit was ₩142.3B, up 371.1%. The report also sees bonding wire use per package rising 50%+ at 12-high LPDDR stacks for SOCAMM2, a tailwind for MK Electron.

If outsourcing share rises while memory output rises, OSAT volumes grow faster than memory bits. That's what a lot of the analysts are saying. They expect the tailwind to last until new commodity DRAM capacity lands in 1H28, so watch Hana Micron's Vietnam ramp and whether Samsung follows with bigger turnkey deals.

原文：DRAM, NAND, AND AI: WHAT KOREAN CHIP RESEARCH REVEALS ABOUT THE SELLOFF

Samsung and SK Hynix just plunged 16.1% and 20.2% in a single week as fears of tighter U.S. data center regulations rattled semiconductor stocks.

But the underlying memory market may be telling a different story.

Korea's Hana Securities argues that the selloff has already priced in much of the regulatory risk, while 2027 earnings forecasts for Korean memory makers are actually being revised higher.

What's particularly valuable is the research behind that conclusion.

Hana's comprehensive 30-page report is packed with detailed charts covering DRAM and NAND spot and contract prices, supply and demand, CAPEX, Korean memory exports, and earnings forecasts.

It's the kind of granular, locally sourced Korean semiconductor research that international investors don't always get to see.

Are investors overreacting to Washington's political uncertainty?

And what do the underlying DRAM and NAND numbers actually tell us about the next phase of the memory cycle?
